   680	        w.start()       # registers, starts heartbeat thread
   681	        try:
   682	            w.run()     # blocks pulling + processing tasks
   683	        finally:
   684	            w.stop()    # graceful: stop claiming, finish in-flight,
   685	                        # release locks, deregister
   686	    """
   687	
   688	    def __init__(self,
   689	                 config: WorkerConfig,
   690	                 *,
   691	                 sandbox_runtime: SandboxRuntime | None = None,
   692	                 agent_executor: AgentExecutor | None = None,
   693	                 gerrit_pusher: GerritPusher | None = None,
   694	                 heartbeat_store: HeartbeatStore | None = None,
   695	                 ) -> None:
   696	        self.config = config
   697	        self.sandbox = sandbox_runtime or LocalSandboxRuntime()
   698	        self.executor = agent_executor or _StubAgentExecutor()
   699	        self.gerrit = gerrit_pusher or StubGerritPusher()
   700	        self.store = heartbeat_store or _get_default_store()
   701	
   702	        self._stop_event = threading.Event()
   703	        self._heartbeat_thread: threading.Thread | None = None
   704	        self._inflight: dict[str, str] = {}        # message_id -> task_id
   705	        self._inflight_lock = threading.Lock()
   706	        self._pending = 0                          # submitted-but-not-started
   707	        self._pending_lock = threading.Lock()
   708	        self._processed: list[WorkerTaskOutcome] = []
   709	        self._processed_lock = threading.Lock()
   710	        self._executor_pool: concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor | None = None
   711	        self._started_at: float = 0.0
   712	        self._signal_handlers_installed = False
   713	
   714	    # ─── lifecycle ───────────────────────────────────────────────
   715	
   716	    def start(self) -> None:
   717	        self._started_at = time.time()
   718	        info = self._info_snapshot(status="starting")
   719	        self.store.register(self.config.worker_id, info,
   720	                            self.config.heartbeat_ttl_s)
   721	        _bump_workers_active(self.store)
   722	        self._executor_pool = concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(
   723	            max_workers=max(1, self.config.capacity),
   724	            thread_name_prefix=f"wkr-{self.config.worker_id}",
   725	        )
   726	        self._heartbeat_thread = threading.Thread(
   727	            target=self._heartbeat_loop,
   728	            name=f"worker-heartbeat-{self.config.worker_id}",
   729	            daemon=True,
   730	        )
   731	        self._heartbeat_thread.start()
   732	        logger.info(
   733	            "worker %s started (capacity=%d, tenant_filter=%s, "
   734	            "capability_filter=%s)",
   735	            self.config.worker_id, self.config.capacity,
   736	            self.config.tenant_filter or "*",
   737	            self.config.capability_filter or "*",
   738	        )
   739	        try:
   740	            metrics.worker_lifecycle_total.labels(event="start").inc()
   741	        except Exception:
   742	            pass
   743	
   744	    def stop(self, *, timeout_s: float = 60.0) -> None:
   745	        """Graceful shutdown.
   746	
   747	        After this returns, ``stop_event`` is set, the heartbeat
   748	        thread has exited, no in-flight task is still claimed, and
   749	        the worker is deregistered from the active set.
   750	        """
   751	        if self._stop_event.is_set():
   752	            return
   753	        self._stop_event.set()
   754	        # Wait for in-flight + pending tasks to drain (run() loop does the work).
   755	        deadline = time.time() + timeout_s
   756	        while True:
   757	            with self._inflight_lock:
   758	                inflight = len(self._inflight)
   759	            pending = self._pending_count()
   760	            if inflight == 0 and pending == 0:
   761	                break
   762	            if time.time() >= deadline:
   763	                logger.warning(
   764	                    "worker %s stop: %d in-flight task(s) still running "
   765	                    "after %.1fs — abandoning",
   766	                    self.config.worker_id, len(self._inflight), timeout_s,
   767	                )
   768	                # Best-effort: nack each in-flight to release the
   769	                # visibility timeout faster + release any locks.
   770	                with self._inflight_lock:
   771	                    in_flight = dict(self._inflight)
   772	                for mid, tid in in_flight.items():
   773	                    self._abandon(mid, tid, "worker stop timeout")
   774	                break
   775	            time.sleep(0.05)
   776	
   777	        if self._executor_pool is not None:
   778	            # We've already drained in-flight tasks above; this is just
   779	            # to release the pool's worker threads.
   780	            self._executor_pool.shutdown(wait=True, cancel_futures=False)
   781	            self._executor_pool = None
   782	        if self._heartbeat_thread is not None:
   783	            self._heartbeat_thread.join(timeout=5.0)
   784	        try:
   785	            self.store.deregister(self.config.worker_id)
   786	        except Exception as exc:
   787	            logger.warning("worker %s deregister failed: %s",
   788	                           self.config.worker_id, exc)
   789	        _bump_workers_active(self.store)
   790	        try:
   791	            metrics.worker_lifecycle_total.labels(event="stop").inc()
   792	        except Exception:
   793	            pass
   794	        logger.info("worker %s stopped (processed=%d)",
   795	                    self.config.worker_id, len(self._processed))
   796	
   797	    def install_signal_handlers(self) -> None:
   798	        """Wire SIGTERM / SIGINT to ``stop()`` for systemd / docker."""
   799	        if self._signal_handlers_installed:
   800	            return
   801	        if threading.current_thread() is not threading.main_thread():
   802	            # signal.signal must be called from main thread.
   803	            return
   804	
   805	        def _handler(signum: int, _frame: Any) -> None:
   806	            logger.info("worker %s caught signal %d — graceful shutdown",
   807	                        self.config.worker_id, signum)
   808	            self._stop_event.set()
   809	
   810	        for sig in (signal.SIGTERM, signal.SIGINT):
   811	            try:
   812	                signal.signal(sig, _handler)
   813	            except (ValueError, OSError):
   814	                # In some embedded runtimes setting signals raises.
   815	                pass
   816	        self._signal_handlers_installed = True
   817	
   818	    # ─── main loop ───────────────────────────────────────────────
   819	
   820	    def run(self) -> list[WorkerTaskOutcome]:
   821	        """Block pulling + processing tasks until ``stop_event`` set
   822	        or ``max_messages`` reached.
   823	
   824	        Returns the per-task outcome list — useful for tests + for
   825	        the systemd ExecStop hook to log a summary.
   826	        """
   827	        n = 0
   828	        while not self._stop_event.is_set():
   829	            if (self.config.max_messages is not None
   830	                    and n >= self.config.max_messages):
   831	                break
   832	            free = self.config.capacity - max(
   833	                self._inflight_count(), self._pending_count(),
   834	            )
   835	            if free <= 0:
   836	                time.sleep(self.config.loop_idle_s)
   837	                continue
   838	            pull_n = min(self.config.effective_pull_count(), free)
   839	            try:
   840	                msgs = queue_backend.pull(
   841	                    self.config.worker_id, count=pull_n,
   842	                    visibility_timeout_s=self.config.visibility_timeout_s,
   843	                )
   844	            except Exception as exc:
   845	                logger.exception("worker %s pull failed: %s",
   846	                                 self.config.worker_id, exc)
   847	                time.sleep(self.config.loop_idle_s)
   848	                continue
   849	
   850	            if not msgs:
   851	                time.sleep(self.config.loop_idle_s)
   852	                continue
   853	
   854	            for msg in msgs:
   855	                if self._stop_event.is_set():
   856	                    # Re-queue immediately by returning the message
   857	                    # to Queued; visibility timeout would do this
   858	                    # eventually but we want the next worker to take
   859	                    # it now (graceful shutdown spec).
   860	                    self._return_to_queue(msg, "worker shutting down")
   861	                    self._record_outcome(self._filter_outcome(
   862	                        msg, "shutdown_returned",
   863	                        "worker shutting down",
   864	                    ))
   865	                    n += 1
   866	                    continue
   867	                if not self._matches_filters(msg):
   868	                    self._return_to_queue(
   869	                        msg, "filter mismatch (tenant/capability)",
   870	                    )
   871	                    self._record_outcome(self._filter_outcome(
   872	                        msg, "nacked",
   873	                        "filter mismatch (tenant/capability)",
   874	                    ))
   875	                    n += 1
   876	                    continue
   877	                # Dispatch into the thread pool so the worker can have
   878	                # up to ``capacity`` tasks in-flight at once (spec:
   879	                # ``--capacity N``).  ``handle()`` never raises so we
   880	                # don't bother chaining a future callback.
   881	                if self.config.capacity == 1 or self._executor_pool is None:
   882	                    self._record_outcome(self.handle(msg))
   883	                else:
   884	                    with self._pending_lock:
   885	                        self._pending += 1
   886	                    self._executor_pool.submit(
   887	                        self._handle_and_record, msg,
   888	                    )
   889	                n += 1
   890	
   891	        # Wait for any remaining submitted tasks (capacity > 1 case).
   892	        # ``_pending`` is bumped at submit() and decremented in
   893	        # ``_handle_and_record`` AFTER the outcome is appended, so
   894	        # waiting on it == waiting for every outcome to be in
   895	        # ``self._processed``.
   896	        if self._executor_pool is not None:
   897	            deadline = time.time() + 60.0
   898	            while self._pending_count() > 0 and time.time() < deadline:
   899	                time.sleep(0.02)
   900	        return list(self._processed)
   901	
   902	    def _handle_and_record(self, msg: QueueMessage) -> None:
   903	        try:
   904	            self._record_outcome(self.handle(msg))
   905	        except Exception as exc:                       # pragma: no cover
   906	            logger.exception("handle(%s) crashed: %s", msg.message_id, exc)
   907	        finally:
   908	            with self._pending_lock:
   909	                self._pending -= 1
   910	
   911	    def _pending_count(self) -> int:
   912	        with self._pending_lock:
   913	            return self._pending
   914	
   915	    def _record_outcome(self, outcome: WorkerTaskOutcome) -> None:
   916	        with self._processed_lock:
   917	            self._processed.append(outcome)
   918	
   919	    # ─── single-task processing ──────────────────────────────────
   920	
   921	    def handle(self, msg: QueueMessage) -> WorkerTaskOutcome:
   922	        """Process one ``QueueMessage`` end-to-end.
   923	
   924	        Returns a ``WorkerTaskOutcome`` audit row.  Never raises:
   925	        every failure path is caught and translated into ``nack``
   926	        (queue handles 3-strike + DLQ) so the worker loop can keep
   927	        going.
   928	        """
   929	        started = time.time()
   930	        task_id = _task_id_for(msg)
   931	        outcome = WorkerTaskOutcome(
   932	            message_id=msg.message_id,
   933	            task_id=task_id,
   934	            jira_ticket=_safe_ticket(msg),
   935	            status="acked",
   936	        )
   937	        # O10 (#273): reject forged / tampered queue payloads *before*
   938	        # touching the sandbox.  When no HMAC key is configured the call
   939	        # is a no-op and we fall back to pre-O10 behaviour.
   940	        try:
   941	            queue_backend.verify_pulled_message(msg)
   942	        except Exception as exc:
   943	            outcome.status = "nacked"
   944	            outcome.error = f"O10 HMAC verify failed: {exc}"
   945	            # Immediate DLQ — a bad signature is not a transient fault and
   946	            # re-queueing it would just poison the next puller.  We NACK
   947	            # MAX_DELIVERIES times to trigger DLQ in one call.
   948	            try:
   949	                for _ in range(queue_backend.MAX_DELIVERIES):
   950	                    queue_backend.nack(
   951	                        msg.message_id, outcome.error,
   952	                        queue_backend.format_exc(exc),
   953	                    )
   954	            except Exception:
   955	                pass
   956	            outcome.elapsed_s = time.time() - started
   957	            try:
   958	                metrics.worker_task_total.labels(outcome="hmac_rejected").inc()
   959	            except Exception:
   960	                pass
   961	            return outcome
   962	        try:
   963	            card = msg.task_card()
   964	        except Exception as exc:
   965	            outcome.status = "nacked"
   966	            outcome.error = f"corrupt CATC payload: {exc}"
   967	            queue_backend.nack(msg.message_id,
   968	                               outcome.error,
   969	                               queue_backend.format_exc(exc))
   970	            outcome.elapsed_s = time.time() - started
   971	            return outcome
   972	
   973	        # ─── distributed lock on impact_scope.allowed ───────────
   974	        allowed_paths = list(card.navigation.impact_scope.allowed)
   975	        lock_res = dist_lock.acquire_paths(
   976	            task_id, allowed_paths, ttl_s=self.config.lock_ttl_s,
   977	            wait_timeout_s=self.config.lock_wait_s,
   978	        )
   979	        if not lock_res.ok:
   980	            outcome.status = "locked_skipped"

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	"""O2 (#265) — Message Queue abstraction layer.
     2	
     3	The orchestrator pushes CATC payloads (O0 / ``backend/catc.py``) here;
     4	stateless workers (O3) pull them, take dist-locks (O1 /
     5	``backend/dist_lock.py``), execute, and ack.  This module hides the
     6	backing technology behind a single ``QueueBackend`` Protocol so the
     7	worker pool, gateway, and tests all see one API.
     8	
     9	Contract:
    10	  * ``push(card, priority=PriorityLevel.P2)`` enqueues a CATC payload as
    11	    a fresh ``QueueMessage`` in state ``Queued``.  The message id is
    12	    minted server-side and returned.
    13	  * ``pull(consumer, count=1, block_ms=0)`` claims up to ``count`` ready
    14	    messages on behalf of a worker.  Each claimed message has a
    15	    *visibility timeout* — if the worker doesn't ``ack`` or ``nack``
    16	    before it elapses, ``sweep_visibility()`` puts the message back on
    17	    the queue (state Queued) so a peer can take it.
    18	  * ``ack(message_id)`` permanently removes the message; it succeeded.
    19	  * ``nack(message_id, reason, stack=None)`` increments
    20	    ``delivery_count``; once it crosses ``MAX_DELIVERIES`` (3) the
    21	    message moves to the DLQ stream with the original CATC plus the
    22	    last failure reason and stack.
    23	  * ``set_state(message_id, state)`` lets the worker / orchestrator
    24	    advance the state machine (``Queued`` → ``Blocked_by_Mutex`` →
    25	    ``Ready`` → ``Claimed`` → ``Running`` → ``Done`` / ``Failed``).
    26	  * ``dlq_list(limit=100)`` / ``dlq_purge(message_id)`` / ``dlq_redrive``
    27	    — operator surface for inspecting and recovering DLQ entries.
    28	
    29	Priority queues:
    30	  * Four classes (``P0`` = incident, ``P1`` = hotfix, ``P2`` = sprint,
    31	    ``P3`` = backlog).  Pull always drains the highest-priority bucket
    32	    first; a P3 caller never starves a P0 sitting in the queue.
    33	
    34	Backend selection:
    35	  * If ``OMNISIGHT_REDIS_URL`` is set + the ``redis`` package imports,
    36	    the Redis Streams backend is used.
    37	  * Otherwise the in-memory backend is used (single-process dev / tests).
    38	  * ``rabbitmq`` / ``sqs`` are reserved names; selecting them raises
    39	    explicitly in the factory until real adapters exist.
    40	  * Both implemented backends share observable semantics; that
    41	    equivalence is the contract the test suite enforces.
    42	"""
    43

    45

    47	import logging
    48	import os

    70	DLQ_PREFIX = "omnisight:queue:dlq:"

    74	#  Enums (priority + state machine)
    75	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

    77	
    78	class PriorityLevel(str, Enum):
    79	    """Ordered low→high importance: P3 backlog, P0 incident."""
    80	
    81	    P0 = "P0"   # incident / outage — drained first
    82	    P1 = "P1"   # hotfix / customer escalation
    83	    P2 = "P2"   # sprint work (default)
    84	    P3 = "P3"   # backlog / cleanup
    85	
    86	    @property
    87	    def rank(self) -> int:
    88	        """0 = highest priority (P0), 3 = lowest (P3)."""
    89	        return {"P0": 0, "P1": 1, "P2": 2, "P3": 3}[self.value]
    90	
    91	    @classmethod
    92	    def ordered(cls) -> list["PriorityLevel"]:
    93	        return [cls.P0, cls.P1, cls.P2, cls.P3]
    94	
    95	
    96	class TaskState(str, Enum):
    97	    """Spec state machine.
    98	
    99	    Edges (allowed transitions):
   100	
   101	      * Queued            -> Blocked_by_Mutex | Ready | Failed
   102	      * Blocked_by_Mutex  -> Ready | Failed
   103	      * Ready             -> Claimed | Failed
   104	      * Claimed           -> Running | Queued (visibility expired) | Failed
   105	      * Running           -> Done | Failed | Queued (visibility expired)
   106	      * Done              -> (terminal — ack required)
   107	      * Failed            -> (terminal — DLQ on Nth failure)
   108	    """
   109	
   110	    Queued = "Queued"
   111	    Blocked_by_Mutex = "Blocked_by_Mutex"
   112	    Ready = "Ready"
   113	    Claimed = "Claimed"
   114	    Running = "Running"
   115	    Done = "Done"
   116	    Failed = "Failed"
   117	
   118	
   119	_ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS: dict[TaskState, set[TaskState]] = {
   120	    TaskState.Queued:           {TaskState.Blocked_by_Mutex, TaskState.Ready,
   121	                                 TaskState.Claimed, TaskState.Failed},
   122	    TaskState.Blocked_by_Mutex: {TaskState.Queued, TaskState.Ready,
   123	                                 TaskState.Failed},
   124	    TaskState.Ready:            {TaskState.Claimed, TaskState.Failed,
   125	                                 TaskState.Queued},
   126	    TaskState.Claimed:          {TaskState.Running, TaskState.Queued,
   127	                                 TaskState.Failed, TaskState.Done},
   128	    TaskState.Running:          {TaskState.Done, TaskState.Failed,
   129	                                 TaskState.Queued},
   130	    TaskState.Done:             set(),
   131	    TaskState.Failed:           set(),
   132	}
   133	
   134	
   135	def _check_transition(old: TaskState, new: TaskState) -> None:
   136	    if old == new:
   137	        return
   138	    if new not in _ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS[old]:
   139	        raise InvalidStateTransition(
   140	            f"illegal queue state transition: {old.value} -> {new.value}"
   141	        )
   142	
   143	
   144	class InvalidStateTransition(ValueError):
   145	    """Raised when ``set_state`` is called with a disallowed edge."""
   146	
   147	
   148	class MessageNotFound(KeyError):
   149	    """Raised when an op references an unknown / already-acked message_id."""
   150	
   151

   156	
   157	@dataclass
   158	class QueueMessage:
   159	    """One enqueued CATC payload + queue-side bookkeeping."""
   160	
   161	    message_id: str
   162	    priority: PriorityLevel
   163	    state: TaskState
   164	    payload: dict[str, Any]                   # CATC TaskCard.to_dict()
   165	    enqueued_at: float
   166	    delivery_count: int = 0
   167	    claim_owner: str | None = None
   168	    claim_deadline: float = 0.0               # unix seconds
   169	    last_error: str | None = None
   170	    last_error_stack: str | None = None
   171	    history: list[tuple[float, str]] = field(default_factory=list)   # (ts, state)
   172	
   173	    def task_card(self) -> TaskCard:
   174	        return TaskCard.from_dict(self.payload)
   175	
   176	    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
   177	        return {
   178	            "message_id": self.message_id,
   179	            "priority": self.priority.value,
   180	            "state": self.state.value,
   181	            "payload": self.payload,
   182	            "enqueued_at": self.enqueued_at,
   183	            "delivery_count": self.delivery_count,
   184	            "claim_owner": self.claim_owner,
   185	            "claim_deadline": self.claim_deadline,
   186	            "last_error": self.last_error,
   187	            "last_error_stack": self.last_error_stack,
   188	            "history": [list(h) for h in self.history],
   189	        }
   190	
   191	    @classmethod
   192	    def from_dict(cls, raw: dict[str, Any]) -> "QueueMessage":
   193	        return cls(
   194	            message_id=raw["message_id"],
   195	            priority=PriorityLevel(raw["priority"]),
   196	            state=TaskState(raw["state"]),
   197	            payload=dict(raw["payload"]),
   198	            enqueued_at=float(raw["enqueued_at"]),
   199	            delivery_count=int(raw.get("delivery_count", 0)),
   200	            claim_owner=raw.get("claim_owner"),
   201	            claim_deadline=float(raw.get("claim_deadline") or 0.0),
   202	            last_error=raw.get("last_error"),
   203	            last_error_stack=raw.get("last_error_stack"),
   204	            history=[tuple(h) for h in raw.get("history", [])],
   205	        )
   206	
   207	
   208	@dataclass
   209	class DlqEntry:
   210	    """A message that exceeded ``MAX_DELIVERIES`` failures."""
   211	
   212	    message_id: str
   213	    priority: PriorityLevel
   214	    payload: dict[str, Any]
   215	    failure_count: int
   216	    root_cause: str
   217	    stack: str | None
   218	    moved_to_dlq_at: float
   219	    enqueued_at: float
   220	
   221	    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
   222	        return {
   223	            "message_id": self.message_id,
   224	            "priority": self.priority.value,
   225	            "payload": self.payload,
   226	            "failure_count": self.failure_count,
   227	            "root_cause": self.root_cause,
   228	            "stack": self.stack,
   229	            "moved_to_dlq_at": self.moved_to_dlq_at,
   230	            "enqueued_at": self.enqueued_at,
   231	        }
   232	
   233	    @classmethod
   234	    def from_dict(cls, raw: dict[str, Any]) -> "DlqEntry":
   235	        return cls(
   236	            message_id=raw["message_id"],
   237	            priority=PriorityLevel(raw["priority"]),
   238	            payload=dict(raw["payload"]),
   239	            failure_count=int(raw["failure_count"]),

   241	            stack=raw.get("stack"),
   242	            moved_to_dlq_at=float(raw["moved_to_dlq_at"]),

  1104	    # ─── helpers ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1105	
  1106	    def _matches_filters(self, msg: QueueMessage) -> bool:
  1107	        if not self.config.tenant_filter and not self.config.capability_filter:
  1108	            return True
  1109	        tenant = _msg_tenant(msg)
  1110	        if self.config.tenant_filter and tenant not in self.config.tenant_filter:
  1111	            return False
  1112	        if self.config.capability_filter:
  1113	            caps = _msg_capabilities(msg)
  1114	            if not any(c in self.config.capability_filter for c in caps):
  1115	                return False
  1116	        return True
  1117	
  1118	    def _filter_outcome(self, msg: QueueMessage, status: str,
  1119	                        reason: str) -> WorkerTaskOutcome:
  1120	        return WorkerTaskOutcome(
  1121	            message_id=msg.message_id,
  1122	            task_id=_task_id_for(msg),
  1123	            jira_ticket=_safe_ticket(msg),
  1124	            status=status,
  1125	            error=reason,
  1126	        )
  1127	
  1128	    def _return_to_queue(self, msg: QueueMessage, reason: str) -> None:
  1129	        """Best-effort: bump delivery_count back without consuming a
  1130	        retry budget by re-queueing via state transition.
  1131	
  1132	        We can't truly "un-claim" a message in O2's contract — but the
  1133	        next ``sweep_visibility`` will do it for us once the deadline
  1134	        passes.  For graceful shutdown / filter mismatch we still
  1135	        prefer to nack with a benign reason so the queue can re-deliver
  1136	        immediately, AT THE COST of one delivery_count tick.  3-strike
  1137	        rule applies — operators shouldn't loop a worker between
  1138	        filters that always reject.
  1139	        """
  1140	        try:
  1141	            queue_backend.nack(msg.message_id, reason)
  1142	        except Exception as exc:
  1143	            logger.warning("return_to_queue(%s) failed: %s",
  1144	                           msg.message_id, exc)
  1145	
  1146	    def _abandon(self, message_id: str, task_id: str, reason: str) -> None:
  1147	        try:
  1148	            queue_backend.nack(message_id, reason)
  1149	        except Exception:
  1150	            pass
  1151	        try:
  1152	            dist_lock.release_paths(task_id)
  1153	        except Exception:
  1154	            pass
  1155	
  1156	    def _track_inflight(self, message_id: str, task_id: str) -> None:
  1157	        with self._inflight_lock:
  1158	            self._inflight[message_id] = task_id
  1159	        try:
  1160	            metrics.worker_inflight.set(self._inflight_count())
  1161	        except Exception:
  1162	            pass
  1163	
  1164	    def _untrack_inflight(self, message_id: str) -> None:
  1165	        with self._inflight_lock:
  1166	            self._inflight.pop(message_id, None)
  1167	        try:
  1168	            metrics.worker_inflight.set(self._inflight_count())
  1169	        except Exception:
  1170	            pass
  1171	
  1172	    def _inflight_count(self) -> int:
  1173	        with self._inflight_lock:
  1174	            return len(self._inflight)
  1175	
  1176	    def _heartbeat_loop(self) -> None:
  1177	        while not self._stop_event.wait(self.config.heartbeat_interval_s):
  1178	            try:
  1179	                self.store.heartbeat(
  1180	                    self.config.worker_id,
  1181	                    self._info_snapshot(status="alive"),
  1182	                    self.config.heartbeat_ttl_s,
  1183	                )
  1184	                try:
  1185	                    metrics.worker_heartbeat_total.inc()
  1186	                except Exception:
  1187	                    pass
  1188	            except Exception as exc:
  1189	                logger.warning(
  1190	                    "worker %s heartbeat failed: %s",
  1191	                    self.config.worker_id, exc,
  1192	                )
  1193	
  1194	    def _info_snapshot(self, *, status: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
  1195	        snap = {
  1196	            "worker_id": self.config.worker_id,
  1197	            "host": socket.gethostname(),
  1198	            "pid": os.getpid(),
  1199	            "status": status,
  1200	            "capacity": self.config.capacity,
  1201	            "tenant_filter": list(self.config.tenant_filter),
  1202	            "capability_filter": list(self.config.capability_filter),
  1203	            "started_at": self._started_at,
  1204	            "inflight": self._inflight_count(),
  1205	            "processed": len(self._processed),
  1206	        }
  1207	        # O10 (#273): advertise the TLS certificate fingerprint so the
  1208	        # orchestrator / dashboard can pin it — if the cert rotates
  1209	        # without an ops approval, the next attestation will fail.
  1210	        fp = os.environ.get("OMNISIGHT_WORKER_TLS_FP", "").strip()
  1211	        if fp:
  1212	            snap["tls_cert_fingerprint"] = fp
  1213	        return snap
  1214

  1242	def _msg_capabilities(msg: QueueMessage) -> list[str]:
  1243	    """Best-effort capability label extraction.
  1244	
  1245	    The CATC schema (O0) doesn't yet have a dedicated ``capabilities``
  1246	    field — for now the worker reads a comma-list out of
  1247	    ``handoff_protocol`` (each entry is treated as a capability tag)
  1248	    + a fallback to a leading ``cap:foo`` token in ``domain_context``.
  1249	    Capability scoping graduates to a first-class field when O5 lands.
  1250	    """
  1251	    caps: list[str] = []
  1252	    proto = msg.payload.get("handoff_protocol") or []
  1253	    if isinstance(proto, list):
  1254	        for item in proto:
  1255	            if isinstance(item, str) and item.startswith("cap:"):
  1256	                caps.append(item[4:])
  1257	    dc = str(msg.payload.get("domain_context", ""))
  1258	    for tok in dc.split():
  1259	        if tok.startswith("cap:"):
  1260	            caps.append(tok[4:])

     1	"""Phase 59 — Host-Native target detection helpers.
     2	
     3	Single source of truth for "is this project a same-arch host-native
     4	build?" + "should we shortcut the pipeline to the 4-phase app_only
     5	flow?". Used by:
     6	
     7	  - decision_engine.propose() injects is_host_native into source so
     8	    chooser confidence can step up
     9	  - forecast.py reads project_track to pick task templates
    10	  - pipeline.py (Phase 59 v2 — out of scope for this commit) will
    11	    branch on app_only_pipeline()
    12	
    13	Reads `hardware_manifest.yaml` lazily; results cached for 60s so the
    14	hot path doesn't hit disk on every decision propose.
    15	"""
    16	
    17	from __future__ import annotations
    18	
    19	import logging
    20	import os
    21	import platform
    22	import time
    23	from pathlib import Path
    24	
    25	import yaml
    26	
    27	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    28	
    29	_PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    30	_CACHE_TTL_S = 60.0
    31	_cache: tuple[float, dict] | None = None
    32	
    33	
    34	def _read_manifest() -> dict:
    35	    """Read + cache hardware_manifest.yaml. Returns {} on any error."""
    36	    global _cache
    37	    now = time.time()
    38	    if _cache and (now - _cache[0]) < _CACHE_TTL_S:
    39	        return _cache[1]
    40	    path = _PROJECT_ROOT / "configs" / "hardware_manifest.yaml"
    41	    if not path.exists():
    42	        _cache = (now, {})
    43	        return {}
    44	    try:
    45	        data = yaml.safe_load(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")) or {}
    46	    except Exception as exc:
    47	        logger.debug("manifest read failed: %s", exc)
    48	        data = {}
    49	    _cache = (now, data)
    50	    return data
    51	
    52	
    53	def _bust_cache() -> None:
    54	    global _cache
    55	    _cache = None
    56	
    57	
    58	_HOST_ARCH_ALIASES = {
    59	    "x86_64": "x86_64", "amd64": "x86_64",
    60	    "aarch64": "arm64", "arm64": "arm64",
    61	    "armv7l": "arm32", "armv7": "arm32",
    62	    "riscv64": "riscv64",
    63	}
    64	
    65	
    66	def host_arch_canonical() -> str:
    67	    raw = platform.machine().lower().strip()
    68	    return _HOST_ARCH_ALIASES.get(raw, raw)
    69	
    70	
    71	def target_platform_id() -> str:
    72	    """The `project.target_platform` value from manifest, or empty."""
    73	    return ((_read_manifest().get("project") or {}).get("target_platform") or "").strip()
    74	
    75	
    76	def project_track() -> str:
    77	    """The `project.project_track` value from manifest, or empty."""
    78	    return ((_read_manifest().get("project") or {}).get("project_track") or "").strip().lower()
    79	
    80	
    81	def is_host_native() -> bool:
    82	    """True iff target_platform == 'host_native', i.e. operator
    83	    explicitly opted into the same-arch fast path. We don't auto-detect
    84	    because some embedded SoCs identify as x86_64 but still need a
    85	    vendor toolchain (e.g. proprietary RT extensions)."""
    86	    return target_platform_id() == "host_native"
    87	
    88	
    89	def should_use_app_only_pipeline() -> bool:
    90	    """True when the project should run the reduced 4-phase pipeline.
    91	    Currently gated on `project_track == 'app_only'`; future logic
    92	    might also enable it for tiny algo-only projects."""
    93	    return project_track() == "app_only"
    94	
    95	
    96	def app_only_phases() -> list[str]:
    97	    """The reduced NPI sequence used when should_use_app_only_pipeline()."""
    98	    return ["concept", "build", "test", "deploy"]
    99	
   100	
   101	def host_device_passthrough() -> str:
   102	    """Returns the configured accelerator passthrough mode for sandbox
   103	    container creation. Empty string means none (default).
   104	    Recognised values: 'hailo' | 'movidius' | 'usb' | 'none' | ''."""
   105	    return (os.environ.get("OMNISIGHT_HOST_DEVICE_PASSTHROUGH", "") or "").strip().lower()
   106	
   107	
   108	def context_dict() -> dict:
   109	    """Convenience: compact dict of host-native flags suitable for
   110	    spreading into decision.source or forecast.from_manifest input."""
   111	    return {
   112	        "is_host_native": is_host_native(),
   113	        "project_track": project_track(),
   114	        "host_arch": host_arch_canonical(),
   115	        "target_platform": target_platform_id(),
   116	        "app_only_pipeline": should_use_app_only_pipeline(),
   117	        "device_passthrough": host_device_passthrough(),
   118	    }

/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba backend/t3_resolver.py | sed -n '1,280p'" in /home/user/work/sora/OmniSight-Productizer
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	"""Phase 64-C-LOCAL S1 — T3 runner resolver.
     2	
     3	Historical T3 design assumed "a remote hardware daemon". That's fine
     4	for Jetson / Rockchip / MCU targets but overkill for the 90% use
     5	case: host == target (AMD 9950X WSL deploying to its own x86_64
     6	Linux). This module is the resolver layer that picks the right
     7	runner class based on arch/OS match + available capability.
     8	
     9	Hierarchy (top first; later SSH/QEMU land in follow-up phases):
    10	
    11	    required_tier=t3 → resolve_t3_runner(task, target_profile)
    12	      ├─ host_arch == target_arch && host_os == target_os → LOCAL  ⭐
    13	      ├─ registered_remote_runner_matches                  → SSH    (64-C-SSH)
    14	      ├─ can_qemu_emulate(target_arch)                     → QEMU   (64-C-QEMU)
    15	      └─ fallback                                          → BUNDLE (current)
    16	
    17	The resolver is consulted by:
    18	  * dag_validator — `tier_violation` rule passes when LOCAL (or any
    19	    live runner) can serve the task, fails otherwise.
    20	  * container.py T3 executor — dispatches to the chosen runner.
    21	  * Ops Summary panel — shows per-runner dispatch counts.
    22	
    23	Opt-out: `OMNISIGHT_T3_LOCAL_ENABLED=false` forces BUNDLE even when
    24	LOCAL would match. Safe default for paranoid deployments; prod use
    25	keeps it enabled so single-arch deploys stay automated.
    26	"""
    27	
    28	from __future__ import annotations
    29	
    30	import logging
    31	import os
    32	import platform as _stdlib_platform
    33	from dataclasses import dataclass
    34	from enum import Enum
    35	from typing import Optional
    36	
    37	from backend.routers.system import _canon_arch
    38	
    39	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    40	
    41	
    42	class T3RunnerKind(str, Enum):
    43	    """What kind of runner will serve this task. String values match
    44	    the metric label + UI chip copy so ops dashboards don't need a
    45	    second enum."""
    46	    LOCAL = "local"
    47	    SSH = "ssh"      # Phase 64-C-SSH
    48	    QEMU = "qemu"    # Phase 64-C-QEMU
    49	    BUNDLE = "bundle"
    50	
    51	
    52	@dataclass(frozen=True)
    53	class T3Resolution:
    54	    kind: T3RunnerKind
    55	    reason: str                 # human-readable "why this runner"
    56	    target_arch: str            # canonical
    57	    target_os: str              # linux|darwin|windows|…
    58	    host_arch: str
    59	    host_os: str
    60	
    61	
    62	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
    63	#  Host introspection
    64	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
    65	
    66	def host_arch() -> str:
    67	    """Canonical host arch. Thin wrapper so tests can monkeypatch."""
    68	    return _canon_arch(_stdlib_platform.machine())
    69	
    70	
    71	def host_os() -> str:
    72	    """Canonical host OS. Linux / darwin / windows; `wsl` folded into
    73	    `linux` because WSL2 runs a real Linux kernel."""
    74	    return _stdlib_platform.system().lower() or "linux"
    75	
    76	
    77	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
    78	#  Resolution
    79	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
    80	
    81	def _ssh_enabled() -> bool:
    82	    """Kill-switch for SSH runner. Default on."""
    83	    raw = (os.environ.get("OMNISIGHT_SSH_RUNNER_ENABLED") or "true").strip().lower()
    84	    return raw in {"1", "true", "yes", "on"}
    85	
    86	
    87	def _local_enabled() -> bool:
    88	    """Kill-switch env. Default on; set `false` to force BUNDLE even
    89	    when LOCAL would match."""
    90	    raw = (os.environ.get("OMNISIGHT_T3_LOCAL_ENABLED") or "true").strip().lower()
    91	    return raw in {"1", "true", "yes", "on"}
    92	
    93	
    94	def native_arch_matches(target_arch: str, target_os: str = "linux") -> bool:
    95	    """Return True iff the host can natively execute the target's
    96	    binaries — same arch, same OS. Empty / unknown target arguments
    97	    intentionally return False: a missing target_arch must not be
    98	    silently treated as "matches host", or an under-specified DAG
    99	    would auto-route to LOCAL and hide its real target requirement.
   100	    """
   101	    if not target_arch or not target_os:
   102	        return False
   103	    return (
   104	        _canon_arch(target_arch) == host_arch()
   105	        and target_os.lower() == host_os()
   106	    )
   107	
   108	
   109	def resolve_t3_runner(
   110	    target_arch: str = "",
   111	    target_os: str = "linux",
   112	) -> T3Resolution:
   113	    """Pick a T3 runner for the given target. Single entry point —
   114	    both the validator (S3) and the executor (S2) go through here so
   115	    their decisions can't drift.
   116	
   117	    Order of preference: LOCAL → (SSH → QEMU in future phases) →
   118	    BUNDLE. Each candidate is checked in turn; the first that's
   119	    available wins.
   120	    """
   121	    h_arch = host_arch()
   122	    h_os = host_os()
   123	    t_arch = _canon_arch(target_arch) if target_arch else ""
   124	    t_os = (target_os or "linux").lower()
   125	
   126	    # 1. LOCAL — same arch, same OS, kill-switch not pulled.
   127	    if _local_enabled() and native_arch_matches(target_arch, target_os):
   128	        return T3Resolution(
   129	            kind=T3RunnerKind.LOCAL,
   130	            reason=f"host ({h_arch}/{h_os}) matches target ({t_arch}/{t_os})",
   131	            target_arch=t_arch, target_os=t_os,
   132	            host_arch=h_arch, host_os=h_os,
   133	        )
   134	
   135	    # 2. SSH — Phase 64-C-SSH. Check if a registered remote runner
   136	    #    matches the target arch.
   137	    if _ssh_enabled() and t_arch:
   138	        from backend.ssh_runner import find_target_for_arch
   139	        ssh_target = find_target_for_arch(t_arch, t_os)
   140	        if ssh_target is not None:
   141	            return T3Resolution(
   142	                kind=T3RunnerKind.SSH,
   143	                reason=(
   144	                    f"host ({h_arch}/{h_os}) ≠ target ({t_arch}/{t_os}); "
   145	                    f"SSH runner registered at {ssh_target.host}"
   146	                ),
   147	                target_arch=t_arch, target_os=t_os,
   148	                host_arch=h_arch, host_os=h_os,
   149	            )
   150	
   151	    # 3. QEMU — deferred (Phase 64-C-QEMU).
   152	
   153	    # 4. BUNDLE — current fallback: package artefact + install.sh,
   154	    #    operator runs on target manually.
   155	    if not t_arch:
   156	        reason = "target arch not specified — cannot match host; producing bundle"
   157	    elif not _local_enabled():
   158	        reason = "OMNISIGHT_T3_LOCAL_ENABLED=false; producing bundle"
   159	    else:
   160	        reason = (
   161	            f"host ({h_arch}/{h_os}) does not match target ({t_arch}/{t_os}); "
   162	            "producing bundle (no SSH/QEMU runner matched)"
   163	        )
   164	    return T3Resolution(
   165	        kind=T3RunnerKind.BUNDLE,
   166	        reason=reason,
   167	        target_arch=t_arch, target_os=t_os,
   168	        host_arch=h_arch, host_os=h_os,
   169	    )
   170	
   171	
   172	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   173	#  Metric bump (Phase 64-C-LOCAL S4 will render this on Ops Summary)
   174	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   175	
   176	def record_dispatch(kind: T3RunnerKind) -> None:
   177	    """Best-effort Prometheus bump. Swallows errors — metric unavailable
   178	    is not a good reason to abort task dispatch."""
   179	    try:
   180	        from backend import metrics as _m
   181	        if hasattr(_m, "t3_runner_dispatch_total"):
   182	            _m.t3_runner_dispatch_total.labels(runner=kind.value).inc()
   183	    except Exception as exc:
   184	        logger.debug("t3 dispatch metric bump failed: %s", exc)
   185	
   186	
   187	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   188	#  Target-profile convenience
   189	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   190	
   191	def resolve_from_profile(profile: Optional[dict]) -> T3Resolution:
   192	    """Resolve against a `configs/platforms/*.yaml` parsed dict
   193	    directly. Handles the legacy profiles (which may not declare
   194	    `target_os`) by defaulting to linux."""
   195	    profile = profile or {}
   196	    # Profiles declare arch under `kernel_arch` (historical) or `arch`.
   197	    # host_native intentionally leaves `kernel_arch` empty so we treat
   198	    # it as "whatever the host is" — resolve to LOCAL unconditionally.
   199	    name = (profile.get("platform") or "").strip().lower()
   200	    if name == "host_native":
   201	        return resolve_t3_runner(host_arch(), host_os())
   202	    arch = profile.get("kernel_arch") or profile.get("arch") or ""
   203	    target_os = profile.get("target_os") or profile.get("os") or "linux"
   204	    return resolve_t3_runner(arch, target_os)

     1	"""Phase 64-C-SSH — paramiko-based remote runner for cross-arch targets.
     2	
     3	When the host arch does not match the target arch, the T3 resolver
     4	selects SSH as the runner kind. This module connects to the remote
     5	board via SSH, syncs workspace files (sftp), executes commands, and
     6	collects output — all within a timeout + heartbeat envelope.
     7	
     8	Credential lookup follows the git_credentials.yaml pattern: a
     9	dedicated ssh_credentials.yaml (or per-platform deploy fields)
    10	provides host, user, key path, and optional port.
    11	"""
    12	
    13	from __future__ import annotations
    14	
    15	import asyncio
    16	import logging
    17	import os
    18	import stat
    19	import threading
    20	import time

    22	from datetime import datetime
    23	from pathlib import Path
    24	from typing import Optional

    26	try:
    27	    import paramiko
    28	except ImportError:

    34	def _require_paramiko():
    35	    """Raise a clear error if paramiko is not installed."""
    36	    if paramiko is None:
    37	        raise ImportError(
    38	            "paramiko is required for SSH runner (C1 remote execution). "
    39	            "Install it with: pip install paramiko\n"
    40	            "Or install OmniSight with SSH extras: pip install -e '.[ssh]'"
    41	        )
    42	
    43	_PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

    46	@dataclass(frozen=True)
    47	class SSHTarget:
    48	    host: str
    49	    port: int = 22
    50	    user: str = "root"
    51	    key_path: str = "~/.ssh/id_ed25519"
    52	    sysroot_path: str = ""
    53	    scratch_dir: str = "/tmp/omnisight"

    58	    agent_id: str
    59	    target: SSHTarget
    60	    created_at: str = field(default_factory=lambda: datetime.now().isoformat())

    68	def _resolve_key_path(raw: str) -> str:
    69	    return str(Path(raw).expanduser().resolve())
    70	
    71	
    72	def _load_ssh_credentials() -> list[dict]:
    73	    """Load SSH target credentials from ssh_credentials.yaml or
    74	    platform profile deploy fields."""
    75	    import yaml
    76	    from backend.config import settings
    77	
    78	    candidates = []
    79	    if settings.ssh_credentials_file:
    80	        candidates.append(Path(settings.ssh_credentials_file).expanduser())
    81	    candidates.append(_PROJECT_ROOT / "configs" / "ssh_credentials.yaml")
    82	    candidates.append(Path("~/.config/omnisight/ssh_credentials.yaml").expanduser())
    83	
    84	    for path in candidates:
    85	        try:
    86	            resolved = path.resolve(strict=False)
    87	            if resolved.exists():
    88	                data = yaml.safe_load(resolved.read_text(encoding="utf-8")) or {}
    89	                targets = data.get("targets", [])
    90	                if isinstance(targets, list):
    91	                    logger.info("Loaded %d SSH targets from %s", len(targets), resolved)
    92	                    return targets
    93	        except Exception as exc:
    94	            logger.warning("SSH credential load from %s failed: %s", path, exc)
    95	
    96	    return []
    97	
    98	
    99	_SSH_CRED_CACHE: list[dict] | None = None
   100	_SSH_CRED_LOCK = threading.Lock()
   101	
   102	
   103	def get_ssh_targets() -> list[dict]:
   104	    global _SSH_CRED_CACHE
   105	    cached = _SSH_CRED_CACHE
   106	    if cached is not None:
   107	        return list(cached)
   108	    with _SSH_CRED_LOCK:
   109	        if _SSH_CRED_CACHE is not None:
   110	            return list(_SSH_CRED_CACHE)
   111	        _SSH_CRED_CACHE = _load_ssh_credentials()
   112	        return list(_SSH_CRED_CACHE)
   113	
   114	
   115	def clear_ssh_credential_cache() -> None:
   116	    global _SSH_CRED_CACHE
   117	    with _SSH_CRED_LOCK:
   118	        _SSH_CRED_CACHE = None
   119	
   120	
   121	def find_target_for_arch(target_arch: str, target_os: str = "linux") -> SSHTarget | None:
   122	    """Find a registered SSH target matching the requested arch/os."""
   123	    from backend.routers.system import _canon_arch
   124	
   125	    wanted = _canon_arch(target_arch)
   126	    for entry in get_ssh_targets():
   127	        entry_arch = _canon_arch(entry.get("arch", ""))
   128	        entry_os = (entry.get("os", "linux")).lower()
   129	        if entry_arch == wanted and entry_os == target_os.lower():
   130	            return SSHTarget(
   131	                host=entry.get("host", ""),
   132	                port=int(entry.get("port", 22)),
   133	                user=entry.get("user", "root"),
   134	                key_path=entry.get("key_path", "~/.ssh/id_ed25519"),
   135	                sysroot_path=entry.get("sysroot_path", ""),
   136	                scratch_dir=entry.get("scratch_dir", "/tmp/omnisight"),
   137	            )
   138	
   139	    # Fallback: check platform profile deploy fields
   140	    try:
   141	        import yaml
   142	        from backend.platform_profile import _NON_PROFILE_FILES  # W0 #274 (renamed in FX.9.3)
   143	        platforms_dir = _PROJECT_ROOT / "configs" / "platforms"
   144	        for yf in platforms_dir.glob("*.yaml"):
   145	            if yf.name in _NON_PROFILE_FILES:
   146	                continue
   147	            data = yaml.safe_load(yf.read_text()) or {}
   148	            profile_arch = _canon_arch(data.get("kernel_arch") or data.get("arch") or "")
   149	            if profile_arch == wanted and data.get("deploy_method") == "ssh":
   150	                host = data.get("deploy_target_ip", "")
   151	                if host:
   152	                    return SSHTarget(
   153	                        host=host,
   154	                        port=int(data.get("deploy_port", 22)),
   155	                        user=data.get("deploy_user", "root"),
   156	                        key_path=data.get("ssh_key", "~/.ssh/id_ed25519"),
   157	                        sysroot_path=data.get("sysroot_path", ""),
   158	                        scratch_dir=data.get("deploy_path", "/tmp/omnisight"),
   159	                    )
   160	    except Exception as exc:
   161	        logger.debug("Platform profile SSH fallback failed: %s", exc)
   162	
   163	    return None

   165	
   166	def _connect(target: SSHTarget) -> "paramiko.SSHClient":
   167	    """Create and return a connected SSH client."""
   168	    _require_paramiko()
   169	    client = paramiko.SSHClient()
   170	    client.set_missing_host_key_policy(paramiko.RejectPolicy())
   171	
   172	    known_hosts = Path("~/.ssh/known_hosts").expanduser()
   173	    if known_hosts.exists():
   174	        client.load_host_keys(str(known_hosts))
   175	
   176	    key_path = _resolve_key_path(target.key_path)
   177	    if not Path(key_path).exists():
   178	        raise FileNotFoundError(f"SSH key not found: {key_path}")
   179	
   180	    key_stat = os.stat(key_path)
   181	    if key_stat.st_mode & (stat.S_IRGRP | stat.S_IROTH):
   182	        raise PermissionError(
   183	            f"SSH key {key_path} has group/other read permissions — "
   184	            "SSH will refuse it. Run: chmod 600 " + key_path
   185	        )
   186	
   187	    client.connect(
   188	        hostname=target.host,
   189	        port=target.port,
   190	        username=target.user,
   191	        key_filename=key_path,
   192	        timeout=15,
   193	        allow_agent=False,
   194	        look_for_keys=False,
   195	    )
   196	    return client
   197	
   198	
   199	def _setup_sandbox(
   200	    client: paramiko.SSHClient,
   201	    target: SSHTarget,
   202	) -> str:
   203	    """Create per-run scratch directory on the remote. Returns the path.
   204	    If sysroot is specified, verify it exists and is read-only."""
   205	    scratch = target.scratch_dir.rstrip("/") + f"/run-{int(time.time())}"
   206	    _exec_sync(client, f"mkdir -p {scratch}")
   207	
   208	    if target.sysroot_path:
   209	        rc, out = _exec_sync(client, f"test -d {target.sysroot_path} && echo OK")
   210	        if "OK" not in out:
   211	            logger.warning("Sysroot %s not found on remote", target.sysroot_path)
   212	        else:
   213	            rc, mount_out = _exec_sync(
   214	                client,
   215	                f"mount | grep '{target.sysroot_path}' | grep -q 'ro,' && echo RO"
   216	            )
   217	            if "RO" not in mount_out:
   218	                logger.warning(
   219	                    "Sysroot %s is not mounted read-only — safety degraded",
   220	                    target.sysroot_path,
   221	                )
   222	
   223	    return scratch
   224	
   225	
   226	def _exec_sync(
   227	    client: paramiko.SSHClient,
   228	    command: str,
   229	    timeout: int = 30,
   230	) -> tuple[int, str]:
   231	    """Execute a command synchronously, return (exit_code, output)."""
   232	    _, stdout, stderr = client.exec_command(command, timeout=timeout)
   233	    out = stdout.read().decode(errors="replace")
   234	    err = stderr.read().decode(errors="replace")
   235	    rc = stdout.channel.recv_exit_status()
   236	    combined = out
   237	    if err:
   238	        combined += f"\n[STDERR] {err}" if out else err
   239	    return rc, combined.strip()
   240	
   241	
   242	async def sync_files_to_remote(
   243	    client: paramiko.SSHClient,
   244	    local_path: Path,
   245	    remote_dir: str,
   246	    *,
   247	    exclude_patterns: list[str] | None = None,
   248	) -> int:
   249	    """Upload workspace files to the remote scratch directory via SFTP.
   250	    Returns the number of files transferred."""
   251	    exclude = set(exclude_patterns or [".git", "__pycache__", ".venv", "node_modules"])
   252	    sftp = client.open_sftp()
   253	    count = 0
   254	
   255	    def _should_exclude(name: str) -> bool:
   256	        return name in exclude
   257	
   258	    def _upload_dir(local: Path, remote: str) -> None:
   259	        nonlocal count
   260	        try:
   261	            sftp.stat(remote)
   262	        except FileNotFoundError:
   263	            sftp.mkdir(remote)
   264	
   265	        for item in sorted(local.iterdir()):
   266	            if _should_exclude(item.name):
   267	                continue
   268	            remote_item = f"{remote}/{item.name}"
   269	            if item.is_dir():
   270	                _upload_dir(item, remote_item)
   271	            elif item.is_file():
   272	                sftp.put(str(item), remote_item)
   273	                count += 1
   274	
   275	    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
   276	    await loop.run_in_executor(None, _upload_dir, local_path, remote_dir)
   277	    sftp.close()
   278	    return count
   279

   280	
   281	async def sync_files_from_remote(
   282	    client: paramiko.SSHClient,
   283	    remote_dir: str,
   284	    local_path: Path,
   285	    *,
   286	    patterns: list[str] | None = None,
   287	) -> int:
   288	    """Download output artifacts from remote to local. Returns file count."""
   289	    sftp = client.open_sftp()
   290	    count = 0
   291	
   292	    def _download_dir(remote: str, local: Path) -> None:
   293	        nonlocal count
   294	        local.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   295	        for attr in sftp.listdir_attr(remote):
   296	            remote_item = f"{remote}/{attr.filename}"
   297	            local_item = local / attr.filename
   298	            if stat.S_ISDIR(attr.st_mode or 0):
   299	                _download_dir(remote_item, local_item)
   300	            else:
   301	                if patterns and not any(
   302	                    attr.filename.endswith(p.lstrip("*")) for p in patterns
   303	                ):
   304	                    continue
   305	                sftp.get(remote_item, str(local_item))
   306	                count += 1
   307	
   308	    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
   309	    await loop.run_in_executor(None, _download_dir, remote_dir, local_path)
   310	    sftp.close()
   311	    return count
   312	
   313	
   314	async def exec_on_remote(
   315	    client: paramiko.SSHClient,
   316	    command: str,
   317	    *,
   318	    timeout: int = 300,
   319	    heartbeat_interval: int = 30,
   320	    max_output_bytes: int = 10_000,
   321	) -> tuple[int, str]:
   322	    """Execute a command on the remote with timeout + heartbeat monitoring.
   323	
   324	    The heartbeat checks that the SSH channel is alive every
   325	    `heartbeat_interval` seconds. If the channel dies or the command
   326	    exceeds `timeout`, the remote process is killed.
   327	    """
   328	    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
   329	
   330	    def _run() -> tuple[int, str]:
   331	        transport = client.get_transport()
   332	        if transport is None:
   333	            return -1, "[ERROR] SSH transport is None"
   334	
   335	        channel = transport.open_session()
   336	        channel.settimeout(float(timeout))
   337	        channel.exec_command(command)
   338	
   339	        output_chunks: list[bytes] = []
   340	        total_bytes = 0
   341	        truncated = False
   342	        start = time.monotonic()
   343	
   344	        while not channel.exit_status_ready():
   345	            elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
   346	            if elapsed > timeout:
   347	                channel.close()
   348	                try:
   349	                    _exec_sync(client, f"kill -9 $(pgrep -f '{command[:40]}')", timeout=5)
   350	                except Exception:
   351	                    pass
   352	                return -1, "[TIMEOUT] Command exceeded {}s limit".format(timeout)
   353	
   354	            if not transport.is_active():
   355	                return -1, "[DISCONNECTED] SSH transport lost"
   356	
   357	            if channel.recv_ready():
   358	                chunk = channel.recv(4096)
   359	                if not truncated:
   360	                    if total_bytes + len(chunk) > max_output_bytes:
   361	                        remaining = max_output_bytes - total_bytes
   362	                        output_chunks.append(chunk[:remaining])
   363	                        truncated = True
   364	                    else:
   365	                        output_chunks.append(chunk)
   366	                total_bytes += len(chunk)
   367	
   368	            time.sleep(min(1.0, heartbeat_interval / 10))
   369	
   370	        while channel.recv_ready():
   371	            chunk = channel.recv(4096)
   372	            if not truncated:
   373	                if total_bytes + len(chunk) > max_output_bytes:
   374	                    remaining = max_output_bytes - total_bytes
   375	                    output_chunks.append(chunk[:remaining])
   376	                    truncated = True
   377	                else:
   378	                    output_chunks.append(chunk)
   379	            total_bytes += len(chunk)
   380	
   381	        stderr_chunks: list[bytes] = []
   382	        while channel.recv_stderr_ready():
   383	            stderr_chunks.append(channel.recv_stderr(4096))
   384	
   385	        rc = channel.recv_exit_status()
   386	        channel.close()
   387	
   388	        out = b"".join(output_chunks).decode(errors="replace")
   389	        err = b"".join(stderr_chunks).decode(errors="replace")
   390	        if err:
   391	            out += f"\n[STDERR] {err}" if out else err
   392	        if truncated:
   393	            out += (
   394	                f"\n[TRUNCATED — {total_bytes} bytes total, "
   395	                f"cap={max_output_bytes}]"
   396	            )
   397	        return rc, out.strip()
   398	
   399	    return await loop.run_in_executor(None, _run)
   400	
   401	
   402	async def run_on_target(
   403	    agent_id: str,
   404	    workspace_path: Path,
   405	    target: SSHTarget,
   406	    command: str,
   407	    *,
   408	    sync_workspace: bool = True,
   409	    sync_back_patterns: list[str] | None = None,
   410	) -> tuple[int, str, SSHRunnerInfo]:
   411	    """Full SSH runner lifecycle: connect → sandbox → sync → exec → collect.
   412	
   413	    Returns (exit_code, output, runner_info).
   414	    """
   415	    from backend.config import settings
   416	    from backend.events import emit_pipeline_phase
   417	
   418	    timeout = settings.ssh_runner_timeout
   419	    heartbeat = settings.ssh_runner_heartbeat_interval
   420	    max_output = settings.ssh_runner_max_output_bytes
   421	
   422	    emit_pipeline_phase(
   423	        "ssh_runner_start",
   424	        f"Connecting to {target.user}@{target.host}:{target.port} for {agent_id}",
   425	    )
   426	
   427	    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
   428	    try:
   429	        client = await loop.run_in_executor(None, _connect, target)
   430	    except Exception as exc:
   431	        logger.error("SSH connect failed for %s: %s", target.host, exc)
   432	        emit_pipeline_phase("ssh_runner_error", f"Connect failed: {exc}")
   433	        info = SSHRunnerInfo(agent_id=agent_id, target=target, status="connect_failed")
   434	        return -1, f"[SSH CONNECT ERROR] {exc}", info
   435	
   436	    info = SSHRunnerInfo(agent_id=agent_id, target=target, status="connected")
   437	    _active_sessions[agent_id] = info
   438	
   439	    try:
   440	        scratch = await loop.run_in_executor(
   441	            None, _setup_sandbox, client, target,
   442	        )
   443	        logger.info("SSH sandbox: %s on %s", scratch, target.host)
   444	
   445	        if sync_workspace:
   446	            emit_pipeline_phase("ssh_sync_upload", f"Syncing workspace to {target.host}")
   447	            file_count = await sync_files_to_remote(
   448	                client, workspace_path, scratch,
   449	            )
   450	            logger.info("Synced %d files to %s:%s", file_count, target.host, scratch)
   451	
   452	        full_cmd = f"cd {scratch} && {command}"
   453	        emit_pipeline_phase("ssh_exec", f"Running: {command[:80]}")
   454	
   455	        rc, output = await exec_on_remote(
   456	            client,
   457	            full_cmd,
   458	            timeout=timeout,
   459	            heartbeat_interval=heartbeat,
   460	            max_output_bytes=max_output,
   461	        )
   462	
   463	        if sync_back_patterns:
   464	            emit_pipeline_phase("ssh_sync_download", "Collecting artifacts")
   465	            dl_count = await sync_files_from_remote(
   466	                client, scratch, workspace_path / ".ssh_output",
   467	                patterns=sync_back_patterns,
   468	            )
   469	            logger.info("Downloaded %d artifacts from %s", dl_count, target.host)
   470	
   471	        info.status = "completed" if rc == 0 else "failed"
   472	        emit_pipeline_phase(
   473	            "ssh_runner_done",
   474	            f"Exit code {rc} on {target.host}",
   475	        )
   476	        return rc, output, info
   477	
   478	    except Exception as exc:
   479	        logger.error("SSH runner error for %s: %s", agent_id, exc)
   480	        info.status = "error"
   481	        emit_pipeline_phase("ssh_runner_error", str(exc))
   482	        return -1, f"[SSH ERROR] {exc}", info
   483	
   484	    finally:
   485	        try:
   486	            client.close()
   487	        except Exception:
   488	            pass
   489	        _active_sessions.pop(agent_id, None)
   490	
   491	
   492	def get_active_session(agent_id: str) -> SSHRunnerInfo | None:
   493	    return _active_sessions.get(agent_id)
   494	
   495	
   496	def list_active_sessions() -> list[SSHRunnerInfo]:
   497	    return list(_active_sessions.values())
   498	
   499	
   500	async def kill_session(agent_id: str) -> bool:
   501	    """Kill an active SSH session."""
   502	    info = _active_sessions.pop(agent_id, None)
   503	    if not info:
   504	        return False
   505	    info.status = "killed"
   506	    logger.info("SSH session killed for %s", agent_id)
   507	    return True

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	"""Phase 67-C S1 — speculative container pre-warm for DAG dispatch.
     2	M5 — multi-tenant safety: per-tenant bucketing + forced /tmp cleanup.
     3	
     4	Engine 3 from `lossless-agent-acceleration.md`: while the orchestrator
     5	is thinking about the next turn, we can already be pulling the
     6	Tier-1 docker image and starting a container for whichever
     7	in-degree-0 task is queued up next. On consume, the caller hands off
     8	the pre-warmed `ContainerInfo` instead of waiting 1-3s for
     9	`start_container` in the hot path.
    10	
    11	Hard invariants (enforced by tests):
    12	
    13	  * Never bypass Phase 64-A image trust. We literally call
    14	    `container.start_container` — the trust check happens inside it.
    15	  * Never exceed Phase 64-D lifetime cap. Pre-warmed containers are
    16	    registered in the same `_containers` dict, so the existing
    17	    `_lifetime_killswitch` watchdog covers them for free.
    18	  * In-degree ≠ 0 tasks are NEVER pre-warmed. A task depending on an
    19	    upstream output has nothing useful a pre-warmed container can
    20	    do yet.
    21	  * Only `depth` most-ready tasks are pre-warmed. The design locked
    22	    this at 2: more is pre-warmed waste, 1 is insufficient when
    23	    dispatch fans out.
    24	  * Cancel releases immediately. Pre-warmed containers that never
    25	    get consumed (e.g., DAG mutation) are stopped right away to
    26	    free the lifetime budget for the replanned run.
    27	  * M5: A pre-warmed container for tenant A cannot be consumed by
    28	    tenant B when policy="per_tenant". The `/tmp` tenant namespace
    29	    is force-cleared on every consume regardless of policy so that
    30	    no cross-run filesystem residue leaks into the real task.
    31	
    32	This module does NOT handle workspace preparation — caller passes a
    33	workspace path when pre-warming, same as `start_container(agent_id,
    34	workspace_path)`.
    35	"""
    36	
    37	from __future__ import annotations
    38	
    39	import asyncio
    40	import logging
    41	import os
    42	from dataclasses import dataclass
    43	from pathlib import Path
    44	from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Optional
    45	
    46	from backend.dag_schema import DAG, Task
    47	
    48	if TYPE_CHECKING:
    49	    from backend.container import ContainerInfo

    51	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

    54	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
    55	#  Policy + depth config
    56	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
    57	
    58	# M5: virtual tenant id used when policy="shared" so all slots share
    59	# the same bucket. Cannot collide with a real tenant id because
    60	# tenant ids are never prefixed with "_".
    61	_SHARED_BUCKET = "_shared"
    62	
    63	_VALID_POLICIES = ("disabled", "shared", "per_tenant")
    64	
    65	
    66	def _prewarm_depth() -> int:
    67	    """Locked default 2. Env override for ops experimentation."""
    68	    raw = (os.environ.get("OMNISIGHT_PREWARM_DEPTH") or "2").strip()
    69	    try:
    70	        return max(0, min(8, int(raw)))
    71	    except ValueError:
    72	        return 2
    73	
    74	
    75	def get_policy() -> str:
    76	    """Return the current prewarm policy: disabled | shared | per_tenant.
    77	
    78	    Reads from settings each call so test monkeypatching works without
    79	    module reimport. Unknown values fall back to ``per_tenant`` so a
    80	    typo never silently degrades tenant isolation.
    81	    """
    82	    try:
    83	        from backend.config import settings as _settings
    84	        raw = (getattr(_settings, "prewarm_policy", "per_tenant") or "").strip().lower()
    85	    except Exception:
    86	        raw = "per_tenant"
    87	    if raw not in _VALID_POLICIES:
    88	        return "per_tenant"
    89	    return raw
    90	
    91	
    92	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
    93	#  Registry (per-tenant bucketed)
    94	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
    95	
    96	@dataclass
    97	class PrewarmSlot:
    98	    """One pre-warmed container awaiting consumption."""
    99	    task_id: str
   100	    agent_id: str  # synthesised: "prewarm-<task_id>-<short>"
   101	    info: "ContainerInfo"
   102	    tenant_id: str = "t-default"  # M5: which tenant owns this slot
   103	
   104	
   105	# Module-level registry: { tenant_id: { task_id: slot } }. A consume()
   106	# pops from the matching tenant bucket only; cancel_all() stops every
   107	# container in one or all buckets.
   108	_prewarmed_by_tenant: dict[str, dict[str, PrewarmSlot]] = {}
   109	_lock = asyncio.Lock()
   110	
   111	
   112	def _reset_for_tests() -> None:
   113	    _prewarmed_by_tenant.clear()
   114	
   115	
   116	def _bucket_key(tenant_id: Optional[str]) -> str:
   117	    """Resolve the dict key for a given tenant under the active policy.
   118	
   119	    - ``disabled``: callers should never reach this; we still normalise
   120	      to a stable key.
   121	    - ``shared``: every call maps to ``_SHARED_BUCKET`` so A and B slots
   122	      co-mingle (legacy behaviour).
   123	    - ``per_tenant``: uses the tenant id itself (or "t-default" if
   124	      context is empty).
   125	    """
   126	    policy = get_policy()
   127	    if policy == "shared":
   128	        return _SHARED_BUCKET
   129	    return (tenant_id or "t-default")
   130	
   131	
   132	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   133	#  DAG walk — in-degree 0 task picker
   134	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   135	
   136	def pick_prewarm_candidates(dag: DAG, *, depth: int | None = None) -> list[Task]:
   137	    """Return up to `depth` Tier-1 tasks with in-degree 0, in DAG
   138	    declaration order so the selection is deterministic (stable
   139	    replay during incident review)."""
   140	    n = depth if depth is not None else _prewarm_depth()
   141	    if n <= 0:
   142	        return []
   143	    # Count in-degree.
   144	    indeg: dict[str, int] = {t.task_id: 0 for t in dag.tasks}
   145	    for t in dag.tasks:
   146	        for d in t.depends_on:
   147	            if t.task_id in indeg:  # self-referential guard
   148	                indeg[t.task_id] = indeg.get(t.task_id, 0) + (1 if d in indeg else 0)
   149	    ready = [t for t in dag.tasks if indeg.get(t.task_id, 0) == 0]
   150	    # Only Tier-1 benefits from the Docker pre-warm; networked/t3
   151	    # paths have different start-up cost profiles we haven't modelled.
   152	    ready = [t for t in ready if t.required_tier == "t1"]
   153	    return ready[:n]
   154	
   155	
   156	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   157	#  H2 row 1515 — host pressure pause
   158	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   159	#
   160	# When the host is high-pressure (CPU/mem/container threshold breached
   161	# OR WSL2 loadavg ratio above HIGH_PRESSURE_LOADAVG_RATIO) we MUST NOT
   162	# launch new pre-warm containers — speculative warmups during overload
   163	# only make the pressure worse. Already-warmed slots are preserved
   164	# untouched: they've already paid their start-up cost, can still be
   165	# consumed, and the H2 backoff in the acquire path provides the
   166	# ultimate gate before they actually run real work.
   167	#
   168	# Reason codes mirror the H2 sandbox.deferred contract for CPU / mem /
   169	# container, plus an extra ``host_loadavg_high`` for the WSL2-only
   170	# auxiliary signal documented on host_metrics.HIGH_PRESSURE_LOADAVG_RATIO.
   171	
   172	PREWARM_PAUSE_REASON_CPU = "host_cpu_high"
   173	PREWARM_PAUSE_REASON_MEM = "host_mem_high"
   174	PREWARM_PAUSE_REASON_CONTAINER = "container_cap"
   175	PREWARM_PAUSE_REASON_LOADAVG = "host_loadavg_high"
   176	
   177	
   178	def _check_host_pressure() -> Optional[str]:
   179	    """Return a reason code if the host is high-pressure, else None.
   180	
   181	    First consults the H2 CPU/mem/container precondition (same source
   182	    of truth as ``_ModeSlot.acquire()`` so prewarm pause and acquire
   183	    deferral never disagree about whether the host is hot). Then falls
   184	    back to the WSL2 loadavg ratio so a host where psutil reports clean
   185	    but the kernel run-queue is saturated still gates new warmups.
   186	
   187	    Failures importing either module return None (allow) — pre-warm is
   188	    an optimisation, never a correctness gate.
   189	    """
   190	    try:
   191	        from backend import decision_engine as _de
   192	        reason = _de._host_precondition_reason()
   193	        if reason:
   194	            return reason
   195	    except Exception as exc:
   196	        logger.debug("prewarm pressure check (decision_engine) failed: %s", exc)
   197	    try:
   198	        from backend import host_metrics as _hm
   199	        if _hm.is_host_high_pressure():
   200	            return PREWARM_PAUSE_REASON_LOADAVG
   201	    except Exception as exc:
   202	        logger.debug("prewarm pressure check (host_metrics) failed: %s", exc)
   203	    return None
   204	
   205	
   206	def _emit_prewarm_paused(
   207	    reason: str,
   208	    *,
   209	    dag_id: str,
   210	    tenant_id: Optional[str],
   211	    candidate_count: int,
   212	) -> None:
   213	    """Best-effort SSE + metrics emit for a prewarm pause decision.
   214	
   215	    Neither bus.publish nor metric bump failures should ever void the
   216	    pause — the precondition itself is the source of truth, the audit
   217	    trail is just observability.
   218	    """
   219	    try:
   220	        from backend import metrics as _m
   221	        _m.prewarm_paused_total.labels(reason=reason).inc()
   222	    except Exception as exc:
   223	        logger.debug("prewarm_paused metric bump failed: %s", exc)
   224	    try:
   225	        from backend.events import bus as _bus
   226	        _bus.publish("sandbox.prewarm_paused", {
   227	            "reason": reason,
   228	            "dag_id": dag_id,
   229	            "tenant_id": tenant_id or "",
   230	            "candidate_count": candidate_count,
   231	        }, tenant_id=tenant_id)
   232	    except Exception as exc:
   233	        logger.debug("sandbox.prewarm_paused SSE publish failed: %s", exc)
   234	
   235	
   236	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   237	#  prewarm_for — launch containers speculatively
   238	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   239	
   240	async def prewarm_for(
   241	    dag: DAG,
   242	    workspace_path: Path,
   243	    *,
   244	    depth: int | None = None,
   245	    starter=None,  # injectable for tests; None → use container.start_container
   246	    tenant_id: Optional[str] = None,  # M5: bucket owner
   247	) -> list[PrewarmSlot]:
   248	    """Launch up to `depth` pre-warmed containers for the in-degree-0
   249	    Tier-1 tasks of `dag`. Runs sequentially so a failure early
   250	    doesn't pre-fire metrics for later starts.
   251	
   252	    Failures during start (e.g., image trust rejection, bridge
   253	    unavailable) are LOGGED and SWALLOWED — pre-warm is an
   254	    optimisation, not a correctness requirement. The normal
   255	    dispatch path still works.
   256	
   257	    M5: ``tenant_id`` is the owning tenant. Under ``per_tenant`` policy,
   258	    each tenant gets its own bucket (depth applies per bucket). Under
   259	    ``shared``, every call lands in a single ``_shared`` bucket. Under
   260	    ``disabled``, this function short-circuits immediately.
   261	
   262	    H2 row 1515: when the host is high-pressure (CPU/mem/container
   263	    thresholds breached or WSL2 loadavg ratio exceeded), this function
   264	    refuses to launch *new* warm-pool containers. Already-warmed slots
   265	    in ``_prewarmed_by_tenant`` are NOT touched — callers can still
   266	    consume what's already warm, and the H2 acquire-path backoff is the
   267	    final gate before the slot is handed off to a real task. Returns
   268	    only the subset of candidates that were already warmed (possibly
   269	    empty), and emits ``sandbox.prewarm_paused`` SSE + audit so ops can
   270	    correlate the warm-pool pause with the underlying pressure event.
   271	    """
   272	    policy = get_policy()
   273	    if policy == "disabled":
   274	        logger.debug("prewarm: policy=disabled — skipping prewarm_for(%s)", dag.dag_id)
   275	        return []
   276	
   277	    bucket = _bucket_key(tenant_id)
   278	    effective_tid = tenant_id or "t-default"
   279	    candidates = pick_prewarm_candidates(dag, depth=depth)
   280	
   281	    pause_reason = _check_host_pressure()
   282	    if pause_reason is not None:
   283	        logger.info(
   284	            "prewarm: host high-pressure (reason=%s) — pausing new warm pool "
   285	            "for dag=%s tenant=%s; %d already-warmed slot(s) preserved",
   286	            pause_reason, dag.dag_id, effective_tid,
   287	            sum(1 for t in candidates
   288	                if t.task_id in _prewarmed_by_tenant.get(bucket, {})),
   289	        )
   290	        _emit_prewarm_paused(
   291	            pause_reason,
   292	            dag_id=dag.dag_id,
   293	            tenant_id=tenant_id,
   294	            candidate_count=len(candidates),
   295	        )
   296	        async with _lock:
   297	            tenant_slots = _prewarmed_by_tenant.get(bucket, {})
   298	            return [tenant_slots[t.task_id] for t in candidates
   299	                    if t.task_id in tenant_slots]
   300	
   301	    if starter is None:
   302	        from backend.container import start_container as starter  # type: ignore
   303	
   304	    out: list[PrewarmSlot] = []
   305	    # M5: mix the tenant id into the agent_id suffix so two tenants
   306	    # prewarming the same DAG don't collide in container.py's
   307	    # _containers registry (single shared dict keyed by agent_id).
   308	    suffix = _short_id(f"{dag.dag_id}|{effective_tid}")
   309	    for t in candidates:
   310	        agent_id = f"prewarm-{t.task_id}-{suffix}"
   311	        async with _lock:
   312	            tenant_slots = _prewarmed_by_tenant.setdefault(bucket, {})
   313	            if t.task_id in tenant_slots:
   314	                logger.debug(
   315	                    "prewarm: %s already slotted under bucket=%s",
   316	                    t.task_id, bucket,
   317	                )
   318	                out.append(tenant_slots[t.task_id])
   319	                continue
   320	        try:
   321	            # Pass tenant_id through to start_container so cgroup labels
   322	            # + OOM attribution + quota gate all use the right tenant.
   323	            info = await _call_starter(starter, agent_id, workspace_path, effective_tid)
   324	        except Exception as exc:
   325	            logger.warning(
   326	                "prewarm_for: start_container for %s failed: %s", t.task_id, exc,
   327	            )
   328	            try:
   329	                from backend import metrics as _m
   330	                _m.prewarm_consumed_total.labels(result="start_error").inc()
   331	            except Exception as exc:
   332	                logger.debug("prewarm metric bump failed: %s", exc)
   333	            continue
   334	        slot = PrewarmSlot(
   335	            task_id=t.task_id, agent_id=agent_id, info=info,
   336	            tenant_id=effective_tid,
   337	        )
   338	        async with _lock:
   339	            _prewarmed_by_tenant.setdefault(bucket, {})[t.task_id] = slot
   340	        out.append(slot)

exec
