    64	class A2ARateLimited(Exception):
    65	    """Raised when a tenant exceeds one A2A AgentCard bucket."""
    66	
    67	    def __init__(self, tenant_id: str, agent_name: str, retry_after_seconds: float) -> None:
    68	        self.tenant_id = tenant_id
    69	        self.agent_name = agent_name
    70	        self.retry_after_seconds = retry_after_seconds
    71	        super().__init__(
    72	            f"Tenant {tenant_id} A2A agent {agent_name} rate limit exceeded; "
    73	            f"retry in {retry_after_seconds:.2f}s"
    74	        )

    77	class A2ARateGate:
    78	    """Per-tenant/per-AgentCard rate gate.
    79	
    80	    Delegates to ``backend.rate_limit``: Redis coordinates prod workers;
    81	    the documented in-memory fallback remains intentionally per-worker
    82	    for local dev and tests.
    83	    """
    84	
    85	    def __init__(
    86	        self,
    87	        config: A2ARateLimitConfig | None = None,
    88	        *,
    89	        key_prefix: str = "a2a:agent",
    90	    ) -> None:
    91	        self.config = config or A2ARateLimitConfig()
    92	        self.key_prefix = key_prefix
    93	
    94	    def check(self, tenant_id: str, agent_name: str) -> None:
    95	        tid = tenant_id or "t-default"
    96	        agent = agent_name or "agent-card"
    97	        from backend.rate_limit import get_limiter
    98	
    99	        allowed, retry_after = get_limiter().allow(
   100	            f"{self.key_prefix}:{tid}:{agent}",
   101	            self.config.capacity,
   102	            self.config.window_seconds,
   103	        )
   104	        if not allowed:
   105	            raise A2ARateLimited(tid, agent, retry_after)

   121	def _hash_jsonable(value: Any) -> str:
   122	    payload = json.dumps(
   123	        value,
   124	        sort_keys=True,
   125	        separators=(",", ":"),
   126	        ensure_ascii=False,
   127	        default=str,
   128	    )
   129	    return hashlib.sha256(payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()

   132	def _derive_public_base_url(request: Request) -> str:
   133	    """Build the externally-facing origin for AgentCard endpoint URLs."""
   134	
   135	    fwd_proto = (request.headers.get("x-forwarded-proto") or "").split(",")[0].strip()
   136	    fwd_host = (request.headers.get("x-forwarded-host") or "").split(",")[0].strip()
   137	    if fwd_proto and fwd_host:
   138	        return f"{fwd_proto}://{fwd_host}"
   139	    return str(request.base_url or "").rstrip("/")

   164	def _coerce_command(body: A2AInvokeRequest) -> str:
   165	    for value in (body.command, body.message, body.task):
   166	        if value:
   167	            return value
   168	    if isinstance(body.input, str) and body.input:
   169	        return body.input
   170	    if isinstance(body.input, dict):
   171	        for key in ("command", "message", "task", "prompt", "text"):
   172	            value = body.input.get(key)
   173	            if value:
   174	                return str(value)
   175	    raise HTTPException(
   176	        status_code=422,
   177	        detail="A2A invoke payload requires command, message, task, or input text",
   178	    )

   209	async def _audit_a2a_event(
   210	    *,

   213	    tenant_id: str,
   214	    actor: str,

   219	    saved = current_tenant_id()

   233	        except Exception:
   234	            pass
   235	    finally:

   245	    retry = max(1, int(exc.retry_after_seconds) + 1)
   246	    raise HTTPException(
   247	        status_code=429,
   248	        detail={
   249	            "reason": "a2a_rate_limited",
   250	            "tenant_id": exc.tenant_id,
   251	            "agent_name": exc.agent_name,
   252	        },
   253	        headers={"Retry-After": str(retry)},
   254	    ) from exc

     1	"""C4 — L4-CORE-03 Embedded product planner agent (#213).
     2	
     3	Deterministic DAG generator for ``embedded_product`` project class.
     4	Takes a HardwareProfile, ParsedSpec, and a skill pack name, then
     5	produces a complete DAG covering BSP → kernel → drivers → protocol
     6	→ app → UI → OTA → tests → docs.
     7	
     8	The planner reads the skill pack's ``tasks.yaml`` as a template,
     9	evaluates per-task ``when:`` conditions against the hardware profile
    10	to decide which tasks to include, resolves inter-task dependencies
    11	via topological sort, and emits a valid ``DAG`` object that passes
    12	``dag_validator.validate()``.
    13	
    14	    dag = plan_embedded_product(spec, hw_profile, "npu-detection")
    15	"""
    16	
    17	from __future__ import annotations
    18	
    19	import logging
    20	import uuid
    21	from collections import deque
    22	from pathlib import Path
    23	from typing import Any, Optional
    24	
    25	import yaml
    26	
    27	from backend.dag_schema import DAG, Task
    28	from backend.hardware_profile import HardwareProfile
    29	from backend.intent_parser import ParsedSpec
    30	
    31	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    32	
    33	_SKILLS_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "configs" / "skills"
    34	_EMBEDDED_BASE = "_embedded_base"
    35	
    36	_TASKS_CACHE: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
    37	
    38	
    39	def _load_tasks_yaml(skill_pack: str) -> list[dict]:
    40	    if skill_pack in _TASKS_CACHE:
    41	        return _TASKS_CACHE[skill_pack]
    42	
    43	    path = _SKILLS_DIR / skill_pack / "tasks.yaml"
    44	    if not path.exists():
    45	        path = _SKILLS_DIR / _EMBEDDED_BASE / "tasks.yaml"
    46	    if not path.exists():
    47	        raise FileNotFoundError(
    48	            f"No tasks.yaml found for skill pack {skill_pack!r} "
    49	            f"or base template at {path}"
    50	        )
    51	    data = yaml.safe_load(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    52	    tasks = data.get("tasks") or []
    53	    _TASKS_CACHE[skill_pack] = tasks
    54	    return tasks
    55	
    56	
    57	def reload_tasks_cache() -> None:
    58	    _TASKS_CACHE.clear()
    59	
    60	
    61	def _evaluate_conditions(
    62	    conditions: dict[str, Any],
    63	    hw: HardwareProfile,
    64	) -> bool:
    65	    """Evaluate a task's ``when:`` block against the hardware profile.
    66	
    67	    Supported keys:
    68	      has_sensor   — True when hw.sensor is non-empty
    69	      has_npu      — True when hw.npu is non-empty
    70	      has_codec    — True when hw.codec is non-empty
    71	      has_display  — True when hw.display is non-empty
    72	      has_usb      — True when hw.usb is non-empty
    73	      has_peripherals — True when hw.peripherals is non-empty
    74	      soc_contains — True when hw.soc contains the substring (case-insensitive)
    75	    """
    76	    for key, expected in conditions.items():
    77	        if key == "has_sensor":
    78	            if bool(hw.sensor) != expected:
    79	                return False
    80	        elif key == "has_npu":
    81	            if bool(hw.npu) != expected:
    82	                return False
    83	        elif key == "has_codec":
    84	            if bool(hw.codec) != expected:
    85	                return False
    86	        elif key == "has_display":
    87	            if bool(hw.display) != expected:
    88	                return False
    89	        elif key == "has_usb":
    90	            if bool(hw.usb) != expected:
    91	                return False
    92	        elif key == "has_peripherals":
    93	            if bool(hw.peripherals) != expected:
    94	                return False
    95	        elif key == "soc_contains":
    96	            if expected.lower() not in hw.soc.lower():
    97	                return False
    98	        else:
    99	            logger.debug("unknown condition key %r — skipping", key)
   100	    return True
   101	
   102	
   103	def _filter_tasks(
   104	    templates: list[dict],
   105	    hw: HardwareProfile,
   106	) -> list[dict]:
   107	    """Return only the tasks whose ``when:`` conditions pass."""
   108	    result: list[dict] = []
   109	    for tmpl in templates:
   110	        when = tmpl.get("when")
   111	        if when is None or _evaluate_conditions(when, hw):
   112	            result.append(tmpl)
   113	    return result
   114	
   115	
   116	def _resolve_dependencies(tasks: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:
   117	    """Topological sort (Kahn's algorithm) + prune dangling deps.
   118	
   119	    If a task depends on another task that was filtered out by
   120	    conditions, the dependency is silently removed (the driver it
   121	    depended on isn't needed). Raises ValueError on cycles.
   122	    """
   123	    task_ids = {t["task_id"] for t in tasks}
   124	    by_id = {t["task_id"]: t for t in tasks}
   125	
   126	    for t in tasks:
   127	        t["depends_on"] = [d for d in (t.get("depends_on") or []) if d in task_ids]
   128	        t["inputs"] = [
   129	            inp for inp in (t.get("inputs") or [])
   130	            if inp.startswith("external:") or inp.startswith("user:")
   131	            or any(inp == by_id[tid]["expected_output"] for tid in task_ids if tid in by_id)
   132	        ]
   133	
   134	    indeg: dict[str, int] = {t["task_id"]: 0 for t in tasks}
   135	    children: dict[str, list[str]] = {t["task_id"]: [] for t in tasks}
   136	    for t in tasks:
   137	        for d in t["depends_on"]:
   138	            children[d].append(t["task_id"])
   139	            indeg[t["task_id"]] += 1
   140	
   141	    queue: deque[str] = deque(tid for tid, deg in indeg.items() if deg == 0)
   142	    ordered: list[str] = []
   143	    while queue:
   144	        n = queue.popleft()
   145	        ordered.append(n)
   146	        for child in children[n]:
   147	            indeg[child] -= 1
   148	            if indeg[child] == 0:
   149	                queue.append(child)
   150	
   151	    if len(ordered) != len(tasks):
   152	        unresolved = [tid for tid, deg in indeg.items() if deg > 0]
   153	        raise ValueError(
   154	            f"Cyclic dependency detected in embedded plan: {unresolved}"
   155	        )
   156	
   157	    return [by_id[tid] for tid in ordered]
   158	
   159	
   160	def _template_to_task(tmpl: dict) -> Task:
   161	    """Convert a tasks.yaml entry to a DAG Task object."""
   162	    return Task(
   163	        task_id=tmpl["task_id"],
   164	        description=tmpl.get("description", ""),
   165	        required_tier=tmpl.get("required_tier", "t1"),
   166	        toolchain=tmpl.get("toolchain", "cmake"),
   167	        inputs=tmpl.get("inputs") or [],
   168	        expected_output=tmpl["expected_output"],
   169	        depends_on=tmpl.get("depends_on") or [],
   170	    )
   171	
   172	
   173	def plan_embedded_product(
   174	    spec: ParsedSpec,
   175	    hw: HardwareProfile,
   176	    skill_pack: str = "",
   177	    *,
   178	    dag_id: Optional[str] = None,
   179	) -> DAG:
   180	    """Generate a complete embedded product DAG.
   181	
   182	    Parameters
   183	    ----------
   184	    spec : ParsedSpec
   185	        The structured product intent.
   186	    hw : HardwareProfile
   187	        Target hardware capabilities.
   188	    skill_pack : str
   189	        Skill pack name under ``configs/skills/``. Falls back to
   190	        ``_embedded_base`` if the pack has no ``tasks.yaml``.
   191	    dag_id : str, optional
   192	        Override for the DAG ID. Auto-generated if omitted.
   193	
   194	    Returns
   195	    -------
   196	    DAG
   197	        A validated DAG with tasks ordered by dependency.
   198	    """
   199	    pack = skill_pack or _EMBEDDED_BASE
   200	    templates = _load_tasks_yaml(pack)
   201	
   202	    filtered = _filter_tasks(templates, hw)
   203	
   204	    ordered = _resolve_dependencies(filtered)
   205	
   206	    tasks = [_template_to_task(t) for t in ordered]
   207	
   208	    if not dag_id:
   209	        soc_slug = hw.soc.replace(" ", "-").lower()[:20] if hw.soc else "generic"
   210	        dag_id = f"embedded-{soc_slug}-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}"
   211	
   212	    return DAG(
   213	        schema_version=1,
   214	        dag_id=dag_id,
   215	        total_tasks=len(tasks),
   216	        tasks=tasks,
   217	    )
   218	
   219	
   220	def get_task_count_by_phase(dag: DAG) -> dict[str, int]:
   221	    """Group tasks by phase prefix for topology inspection."""
   222	    phases: dict[str, int] = {}
   223	    for t in dag.tasks:
   224	        prefix = t.task_id.split("-")[0]
   225	        phases[prefix] = phases.get(prefix, 0) + 1
   226	    return phases
   227	
   228	
   229	def get_dependency_depth(dag: DAG) -> int:
   230	    """Return the longest dependency chain length (critical path)."""
   231	    by_id = {t.task_id: t for t in dag.tasks}
   232	    depth_cache: dict[str, int] = {}
   233	
   234	    def _depth(tid: str) -> int:
   235	        if tid in depth_cache:
   236	            return depth_cache[tid]
   237	        task = by_id.get(tid)
   238	        if not task or not task.depends_on:
   239	            depth_cache[tid] = 0
   240	            return 0
   241	        d = 1 + max(_depth(dep) for dep in task.depends_on)
   242	        depth_cache[tid] = d
   243	        return d
   244	
   245	    if not dag.tasks:
   246	        return 0
   247	    return max(_depth(t.task_id) for t in dag.tasks)

     1	"""Docker Container Manager for agent isolated execution.
     2	
     3	Layer 2 of workspace isolation: each agent can optionally run commands
     4	inside a Docker container with its worktree mounted as /workspace.
     5	
     6	This provides:
     7	  - Fully isolated toolchain (aarch64 cross-compiler, kernel headers)
     8	  - Clean environment per agent (no host pollution)
     9	  - Container destroyed after cleanup (no residual state)
    10	
    11	Integration with Layer 1 (workspace.py):
    12	  - workspace.py creates the git worktree on the host
    13	  - container.py mounts that worktree into a Docker container
    14	  - tools.py detects if a container is active and routes commands through it
    15	"""
    16	
    17	from __future__ import annotations
    18	
    19	import asyncio
    20	import logging
    21	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    22	from datetime import datetime
    23	from pathlib import Path
    24	
    25	import hashlib
    26	
    27	from backend.events import emit_agent_update, emit_pipeline_phase, emit_container
    28	from backend import metrics as _m
    29	
    30	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    31	
    32	_PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    33	_DOCKERFILE = _PROJECT_ROOT / "backend" / "docker" / "Dockerfile.agent"
    34	
    35	
    36	def _dockerfile_hash() -> str:
    37	    """SHA256 of Dockerfile content → deterministic image tag."""
    38	    try:
    39	        return hashlib.sha256(_DOCKERFILE.read_bytes()).hexdigest()[:12]
    40	    except FileNotFoundError:
    41	        return "latest"
    42	
    43	
    44	DOCKER_IMAGE = f"omnisight-agent:{_dockerfile_hash()}"
    45	DOCKER_TIMEOUT = 60  # seconds for commands
    46	BUILD_TIMEOUT = 300  # seconds for image build
    47	
    48	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
    49	#  M1: DRF token → cgroup hard-limit mapping
    50	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
    51	#
    52	# 1 DRF token (I6 SandboxCostWeight unit) ≈ 1 CPU core × 512 MiB RAM.
    53	# We translate the per-tenant budget acquired from sandbox_capacity into
    54	# concrete docker flags so the kernel — not just the scheduler — enforces
    55	# the share. `--cpus` is the CFS quota cap, `--cpu-shares` becomes the
    56	# cgroup v2 `cpu.weight` proportional share under contention, and
    57	# `--memory` is the hard OOM trigger.
    58	#
    59	# Both bounds clamp to sane safety rails so a buggy caller can't ask for
    60	# 10 000 cores; defaults preserve the legacy `_settings.docker_*_limit`
    61	# behaviour when no tenant_budget is supplied (callers that haven't been
    62	# updated yet still work).
    63	M1_TOKEN_CPU = 1.0          # 1 token → 1.0 CPU core
    64	M1_TOKEN_MEM_MB = 512       # 1 token → 512 MiB
    65	M1_TOKEN_SHARES = 1024      # 1 token → 1024 cpu-shares (Docker default)
    66	M1_MAX_TOKENS = 12.0        # safety clamp; matches CAPACITY_MAX
    67	M1_MIN_TOKENS = 0.25        # never starve below 0.25 token (256 MiB / 0.25 cpu)
    68	
    69	
    70	def _compute_resource_limits(
    71	    tenant_budget: float | None,
    72	) -> tuple[str, str, int]:
    73	    """Translate DRF token budget → (--cpus, --memory, --cpu-shares).
    74	
    75	    `tenant_budget` is the SandboxCostWeight value the I6 layer accepted
    76	    on this tenant's behalf (1.0 lightweight, 4.0 compile, …). When None
    77	    we fall back to the legacy settings.docker_{cpu,memory}_limit so the
    78	    pre-M1 callsites don't change behaviour.
    79	    """
    80	    from backend.config import settings as _settings
    81	
    82	    if tenant_budget is None or tenant_budget <= 0:
    83	        # Legacy path — keep current behaviour. cpu-shares stays at the
    84	        # Docker default (1024), so unbudgeted containers don't get an
    85	        # unfair edge over budgeted ones.
    86	        return (
    87	            str(_settings.docker_cpu_limit or "2"),
    88	            str(_settings.docker_memory_limit or "1g"),
    89	            1024,
    90	        )
    91	
    92	    tokens = max(M1_MIN_TOKENS, min(float(tenant_budget), M1_MAX_TOKENS))
    93	    cpus = f"{tokens * M1_TOKEN_CPU:.2f}"
    94	    mem_mib = int(round(tokens * M1_TOKEN_MEM_MB))
    95	    mem = f"{mem_mib}m"
    96	    shares = max(2, int(round(tokens * M1_TOKEN_SHARES)))
    97	    return cpus, mem, shares
    98	
    99	
   100	# Phase 64-A S1: cache for runtime probe. None = not probed yet.
   101	_RUNTIME_RESOLVED: str | None = None
   102	
   103	
   104	def _is_production_env() -> bool:
   105	    """True only for the deployment mode that must fail closed on gVisor."""
   106	    from backend.config import settings as _settings
   107	    return (_settings.env or "").strip().lower() == "production"
   108	
   109	
   110	async def _detect_available_runtimes() -> set[str]:
   111	    """Return the set of OCI runtimes registered with the local docker.
   112	
   113	    Parses `docker info --format '{{json .Runtimes}}'`. On any failure we
   114	    return {"runc"} since that's the daemon default.
   115	    """
   116	    rc, out, _ = await _run(
   117	        "docker info --format '{{json .Runtimes}}'", timeout=10,
   118	    )
   119	    if rc != 0 or not out:
   120	        return {"runc"}
   121	    try:
   122	        import json
   123	        data = json.loads(out)
   124	        if isinstance(data, dict):
   125	            return set(data.keys())
   126	    except Exception as exc:
   127	        logger.debug("runtime probe parse failed: %s", exc)
   128	    return {"runc"}
   129	
   130	
   131	async def resolve_runtime(force_redetect: bool = False) -> str:
   132	    """Resolve the docker runtime to use, honouring the configured
   133	    preference but falling back to runc when the preferred runtime
   134	    isn't installed. Cached after first call (idempotent for callers)."""
   135	    global _RUNTIME_RESOLVED
   136	    if _RUNTIME_RESOLVED is not None and not force_redetect:
   137	        if _is_production_env() and _RUNTIME_RESOLVED != "runsc":
   138	            raise RuntimeError(
   139	                "ENV=production requires cached sandbox runtime to be runsc; "
   140	                f"got {_RUNTIME_RESOLVED!r}",
   141	            )
   142	        return _RUNTIME_RESOLVED
   143	    from backend.config import settings as _settings
   144	    preferred = (_settings.docker_runtime or "runc").strip().lower()
   145	    if preferred not in {"runsc", "runc"}:
   146	        if _is_production_env():
   147	            raise RuntimeError(
   148	                "ENV=production requires OMNISIGHT_DOCKER_RUNTIME=runsc; "
   149	                f"got {preferred!r}",
   150	            )
   151	        logger.warning(
   152	            "OMNISIGHT_DOCKER_RUNTIME=%r not in {runsc,runc}; using runc",
   153	            preferred,
   154	        )
   155	        _RUNTIME_RESOLVED = "runc"
   156	        return _RUNTIME_RESOLVED
   157	    if _is_production_env() and preferred != "runsc":
   158	        raise RuntimeError(
   159	            "ENV=production requires OMNISIGHT_DOCKER_RUNTIME=runsc. "
   160	            "Use non-production env for explicit runc compatibility tests.",
   161	        )
   162	    available = await _detect_available_runtimes()
   163	    if preferred in available:
   164	        _RUNTIME_RESOLVED = preferred
   165	    else:
   166	        if _is_production_env():
   167	            raise RuntimeError(
   168	                "ENV=production requires gVisor runsc registered with "
   169	                f"Docker; available runtimes={sorted(available)}",
   170	            )
   171	        if preferred != "runc":
   172	            logger.warning(
   173	                "Tier-1 sandbox runtime %r not registered with docker "
   174	                "(available=%s); falling back to runc — escape-resistance "
   175	                "DOWNGRADED. Install gVisor for production.",
   176	                preferred, sorted(available),
   177	            )
   178	            try:
   179	                from backend.events import emit_pipeline_phase as _emit
   180	                _emit("sandbox_runtime_fallback",
   181	                      "runsc unavailable; using runc")
   182	            except Exception:
   183	                pass
   184	        _RUNTIME_RESOLVED = "runc"
   185	    return _RUNTIME_RESOLVED
   186	
   187	
   188	async def _assert_container_runtime(container_name: str, expected: str) -> None:
   189	    """Verify Docker actually launched the sandbox with the requested runtime.
   190	
   191	    In production this is the request-time guard that makes Tier 1 gVisor
   192	    load-bearing instead of trusting an env knob or a cached docker-info probe.
   193	    """
   194	    if expected != "runsc" or not _is_production_env():
   195	        return
   196	    rc, out, err = await _run(
   197	        f"docker inspect --format '{{{{.HostConfig.Runtime}}}}' {container_name}",
   198	        timeout=10,
   199	    )
   200	    actual = (out or "").strip()
   201	    if rc != 0 or actual != expected:
   202	        await _run(f"docker rm -f {container_name} 2>/dev/null", timeout=15)
   203	        raise RuntimeError(
   204	            "Tier-1 sandbox runtime assertion failed: expected runsc, "
   205	            f"got {actual or '<empty>'}; docker inspect error={err or '<none>'}",
   206	        )
   207	
   208	
   209	def _reset_runtime_cache_for_tests() -> None:
   210	    """Test hook — clear the cached runtime so monkeypatched envs apply."""
   211	    global _RUNTIME_RESOLVED
   212	    _RUNTIME_RESOLVED = None
   213

   216	#  Phase 64-A S3: Image digest allow-list
   217	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   218	
   219	class ImageNotTrusted(RuntimeError):
   220	    """Raised when the agent image's sha256 digest is not in the
   221	    configured allow-list."""
   222	
   223	
   224	def _parse_allowed_digests(raw: str) -> set[str]:
   225	    """CSV of `sha256:...` digests → normalised set. Strips whitespace,
   226	    rejects entries that don't look like sha256."""
   227	    out: set[str] = set()
   228	    for item in (raw or "").split(","):
   229	        item = item.strip().lower()
   230	        if not item:
   231	            continue
   232	        if not item.startswith("sha256:") or len(item) != 7 + 64:
   233	            logger.warning(
   234	                "ignoring malformed digest in allow-list: %r "
   235	                "(expected 'sha256:' + 64 hex chars)", item,
   236	            )
   237	            continue
   238	        out.add(item)
   239	    return out
   240	
   241	
   242	async def _inspect_image_digest(image: str) -> str | None:
   243	    """Return the local image's `sha256:...` digest, or None if the image
   244	    is missing / inspect fails. We use `.Id` (the local content digest),
   245	    not `.RepoDigests` (which is registry-specific) — this catches a
   246	    mutated layer even if someone keeps the same tag.
   247	    """
   248	    rc, out, err = await _run(
   249	        f"docker image inspect --format '{{{{.Id}}}}' {image}", timeout=10,
   250	    )
   251	    if rc != 0:
   252	        logger.debug("image inspect failed for %s: %s", image, err or out)
   253	        return None
   254	    digest = out.strip().lower()
   255	    return digest or None
   256

backend/routers/invoke.py:1670:- missing_toolchain:<entry-id>: the operator's INVOKE command (and/or the backlog tasks they queued) will need a vendor toolchain that this machine has not installed yet. The context block hands you the human display name (e.g. "Android SDK Platform Tools", "ESP-IDF v5", "Node.js LTS 20", "Python toolchain (uv)", "ARM GNU Toolchain 13"), a one-line hint about what the toolchain is for, and a one-click install URL of shape `/settings/platforms?entry=<entry-id>`. Surface each missing toolchain as its own bullet, render the install URL as a markdown link with a bilingual action label like `[安裝 / Install](url)` so a CJK or English operator both see a clear CTA, and ALWAYS use the display name — never paste the slug verbatim.
backend/routers/invoke.py:1677:- `missing_toolchain` always leads. The operator already declared intent by typing the command, so install-first-then-run is the productive path; SKIP the `empty_workspace` framing entirely whenever any `missing_toolchain` is present.
backend/routers/invoke.py:1678:- If `stale_pep` co-fires with `missing_toolchain`, mention pending PEPs as ONE short reminder line at the end (not a full sub-list) — the toolchain install is the headline.
backend/routers/invoke.py:1679:- `url_in_message` lands BETWEEN `missing_toolchain` and `empty_workspace`: when a URL is present and no toolchain is missing, lead with the URL menu (the operator declared intent — pick a capability for the URL); SKIP the `empty_workspace` framing in that case for the same reason missing-toolchain skips it. If both `missing_toolchain` AND `url_in_message` co-fire, the toolchain install still leads (cannot run any of the W11/W12/W13 capabilities without it) — append the URL menu as a secondary section after the install link.
backend/routers/invoke.py:1680:- `image_in_message` mirrors `url_in_message` priority: leads when only it + `empty_workspace` co-fire (operator pasted intent); appended as a secondary section when `missing_toolchain` co-fires (install first then run vision pass).    262	async def _lifetime_killswitch(agent_id: str, container_name: str,
   263	                               lifetime_s: float, *, tier: str = "t1") -> None:
   264	    """Wait `lifetime_s`, then force-kill the container if it's still
   265	    in our registry. Cancelled cleanly when the agent finishes via the
   266	    normal stop_container path."""
   267	    try:
   268	        await asyncio.sleep(lifetime_s)
   269	    except asyncio.CancelledError:
   270	        return  # agent finished normally — nothing to do
   271	    info = _containers.get(agent_id)
   272	    if not info or info.container_name != container_name:
   273	        return  # already removed / replaced
   274	    logger.warning(
   275	        "[SANDBOX KILL] %s exceeded lifetime cap %.0fs — SIGKILL",
   276	        container_name, lifetime_s,
   277	    )
   278	    info.status = "killed_lifetime"
   279	    # Best-effort force-remove. We don't await stop_container here
   280	    # because it would re-trigger this same task's cancellation logic;
   281	    # do the docker call directly and pop the registry.
   282	    await _run(f"docker rm -f {container_name} 2>/dev/null", timeout=15)
   283	    _containers.pop(agent_id, None)
   284	    # Audit + metric (best-effort).
   285	    try:
   286	        from backend import audit as _audit
   287	        await _audit.log(
   288	            action="sandbox_killed",
   289	            entity_kind="container",
   290	            entity_id=container_name,
   291	            after={"reason": "lifetime", "tier": tier,
   292	                   "lifetime_s": int(lifetime_s)},
   293	            actor="system:lifetime-watchdog",
   294	        )
   295	    except Exception as exc:
   296	        logger.debug("audit log for sandbox_killed failed: %s", exc)
   297	    try:
   298	        _m.sandbox_lifetime_killed_total.labels(tier=tier).inc()
   299	    except Exception:
   300	        pass
   301	    try:
   302	        emit_container(agent_id, "killed", f"{container_name} (lifetime cap)")
   303	        emit_agent_update(
   304	            agent_id, "error",
   305	            f"Container killed after {int(lifetime_s)}s lifetime cap",
   306	        )
   307	    except Exception:
   308	        pass
   309	
   310	
   311	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   312	#  M1: OOM watchdog
   313	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   314	
   315	OOM_POLL_INTERVAL_S = float("0.5")  # how often to inspect a (still-running) container
   316	OOM_POLL_TIMEOUT = 8                # seconds for a single docker inspect
   317	
   318	
   319	async def _oom_watchdog(agent_id: str, container_name: str,
   320	                        tenant_id: str, *, tier: str = "t1",
   321	                        memory_limit: str = "") -> None:
   322	    """Poll `docker inspect .State` until the container exits, then
   323	    record an audit + metric if it was OOM-killed.
   324	
   325	    The watchdog deliberately polls instead of `docker events` so that:
   326	      * we don't share a single global event stream across containers
   327	        (one bad event-stream consumer breaks all watchdogs);
   328	      * cancel-on-stop is trivial (just `task.cancel()`);
   329	      * we work even when the daemon is rate-limiting events.
   330	
   331	    Cancelled cleanly when stop_container removes the container.
   332	    """
   333	    try:
   334	        while True:
   335	            try:
   336	                await asyncio.sleep(OOM_POLL_INTERVAL_S)
   337	            except asyncio.CancelledError:
   338	                return  # caller stopped us → done
   339	            # If the registry no longer tracks this agent (test reset,
   340	            # crash recovery wipe, etc.) the watchdog has nothing to
   341	            # attribute to — exit instead of spinning.
   342	            current = _containers.get(agent_id)
   343	            if current is None or current.container_name != container_name:
   344	                return
   345	            rc, out, _ = await _run(
   346	                f"docker inspect --format "
   347	                f"'{{{{.State.Status}}}}|{{{{.State.OOMKilled}}}}|"
   348	                f"{{{{.State.ExitCode}}}}' {container_name}",
   349	                timeout=OOM_POLL_TIMEOUT,
   350	            )
   351	            if rc != 0:
   352	                # container removed (exit + auto-rm or stop_container ran)
   353	                # — in either case we can't read the OOM bit any more.
   354	                return
   355	            parts = out.strip().split("|")
   356	            if len(parts) < 3:
   357	                continue
   358	            status, oom_str, exit_str = parts[0], parts[1].lower(), parts[2]
   359	            if status not in ("exited", "dead"):
   360	                continue
   361	            # Reached terminal state — decide if it was an OOM.
   362	            oom_killed = oom_str == "true"
   363	            # Some kernels don't set OOMKilled but still SIGKILL via
   364	            # cgroup memory.events; exit code 137 = 128 + SIGKILL.
   365	            try:
   366	                exit_code = int(exit_str)
   367	            except ValueError:
   368	                exit_code = 0
   369	            if not oom_killed and exit_code == 137:
   370	                # Best-effort: re-check via memory.events oom counter.
   371	                oom_killed = await _read_cgroup_oom_count(container_name) > 0
   372	            if oom_killed:
   373	                await _record_sandbox_oom(
   374	                    agent_id, container_name, tenant_id,
   375	                    tier=tier, memory_limit=memory_limit,
   376	                    exit_code=exit_code,
   377	                )
   378	            return
   379	    except asyncio.CancelledError:
   380	        return
   381	    except Exception as exc:  # never let the watchdog crash silently
   382	        logger.debug("oom watchdog for %s aborted: %s", container_name, exc)
   383	
   384	
   385	async def _cancel_watchdog_task(task) -> None:
   386	    """Cancel and drain a per-container watchdog task."""
   387	    if task is asyncio.current_task():
   388	        return
   389	    try:
   390	        task.cancel()
   391	    except Exception as exc:
   392	        logger.debug("watchdog task cancel failed: %s", exc)
   393	        return
   394	    try:
   395	        await task
   396	    except asyncio.CancelledError:
   397	        pass
   398	    except Exception as exc:
   399	        logger.debug("watchdog task exited during cancellation: %s", exc)
   400	
   401	
   402	async def _read_cgroup_oom_count(container_name: str) -> int:
   403	    """Best-effort read of the kernel oom counter from the container's
   404	    cgroup. Returns 0 on any failure (cgroup gone, permission denied,
   405	    cgroup v1, etc.) — we'd rather under-report than crash."""
   406	    rc, cid, _ = await _run(
   407	        f"docker inspect --format '{{{{.Id}}}}' {container_name}", timeout=5,
   408	    )
   409	    if rc != 0 or not cid.strip():
   410	        return 0
   411	    cid = cid.strip().strip("'\"")
   412	    # cgroup v2 path under systemd-managed docker:
   413	    candidate = Path(
   414	        f"/sys/fs/cgroup/system.slice/docker-{cid}.scope/memory.events"
   415	    )
   416	    if not candidate.is_file():
   417	        # rootless / cgroupns / non-systemd layouts vary; try the
   418	        # generic v2 path that the daemon writes when in cgroupfs mode.
   419	        candidate = Path(f"/sys/fs/cgroup/docker/{cid}/memory.events")
   420	    if not candidate.is_file():
   421	        return 0
   422	    try:
   423	        for line in candidate.read_text().splitlines():
   424	            if line.startswith("oom_kill "):
   425	                return int(line.split()[1])
   426	    except Exception:
   427	        return 0
   428	    return 0
   429	
   430	
   431	async def _record_sandbox_oom(agent_id: str, container_name: str,
   432	                              tenant_id: str, *, tier: str,
   433	                              memory_limit: str, exit_code: int) -> None:
   434	    """Atomic side-effects for a confirmed OOM kill: metric, audit row,
   435	    SSE event, in-memory status bump. Each side-effect is best-effort
   436	    so a single failure (e.g., audit table missing in tests) doesn't
   437	    swallow the others."""
   438	    info = _containers.get(agent_id)
   439	    if info is not None and info.container_name == container_name:
   440	        info.status = "killed_oom"
   441	    logger.warning(
   442	        "[SANDBOX OOM] %s (tenant=%s, mem=%s) — kernel OOM-killer fired",
   443	        container_name, tenant_id, memory_limit,
   444	    )
   445	    try:
   446	        _m.sandbox_oom_total.labels(tenant_id=tenant_id, tier=tier).inc()
   447	    except Exception:
   448	        pass
   449	    try:
   450	        from backend import audit as _audit
   451	        await _audit.log(
   452	            action="sandbox.oom",
   453	            entity_kind="container",
   454	            entity_id=container_name,
   455	            after={
   456	                "agent_id": agent_id,
   457	                "tenant_id": tenant_id,
   458	                "tier": tier,
   459	                "memory_limit": memory_limit,
   460	                "exit_code": exit_code,
   461	                "reason": "cgroup_oom_killer",
   462	            },
   463	            actor="system:oom-watchdog",
   464	        )
   465	    except Exception as exc:
   466	        logger.debug("audit log for sandbox.oom failed: %s", exc)
   467	    try:
   468	        emit_container(agent_id, "oom_killed",
   469	                       f"{container_name} (tenant={tenant_id}, mem={memory_limit})")
   470	        emit_agent_update(
   471	            agent_id, "error",
   472	            f"Sandbox OOM-killed (tenant={tenant_id}, memory={memory_limit})",
   473	        )
   474	    except Exception:
   475	        pass
   476	
   477	
   478	async def assert_image_trusted(image: str = DOCKER_IMAGE) -> None:
   479	    """Reject launch if the configured allow-list is non-empty and the
   480	    image's digest isn't in it. Empty allow-list = open mode (today's
   481	    behaviour, doesn't break dev)."""
   482	    from backend.config import settings as _settings
   483	    allowed = _parse_allowed_digests(_settings.docker_image_allowed_digests or "")
   484	    if not allowed:
   485	        return  # open mode — explicit decision by operator
   486	    digest = await _inspect_image_digest(image)
   487	    if digest is None:
   488	        logger.warning(
   489	            "cannot resolve digest for %s — refusing launch under "
   490	            "strict allow-list mode", image,
   491	        )
   492	        try:
   493	            _m.sandbox_image_rejected_total.labels(image=image).inc()
   494	        except Exception:
   495	            pass
   496	        raise ImageNotTrusted(
   497	            f"image {image!r}: digest unresolvable, cannot verify trust"
   498	        )
   499	    if digest not in allowed:
   500	        logger.error(
   501	            "image %s digest %s NOT in OMNISIGHT_DOCKER_IMAGE_ALLOWED_DIGESTS — "
   502	            "refusing launch (possible tampering or stale image)",
   503	            image, digest,
   504	        )
   505	        try:
   506	            _m.sandbox_image_rejected_total.labels(image=image).inc()
   507	        except Exception:
   508	            pass
   509	        raise ImageNotTrusted(
   510	            f"image {image!r} digest {digest} not in trust list"
   511	        )
   512	    logger.debug("image %s trusted (digest %s)", image, digest)
   513	
   514	
   515	@dataclass
   516	class ContainerInfo:
   517	    """Tracks a running agent container."""
   518	    agent_id: str
   519	    container_id: str
   520	    container_name: str
   521	    workspace_path: Path
   522	    image: str
   523	    created_at: str = field(default_factory=lambda: datetime.now().isoformat())
   524	    status: str = "running"  # running | stopped | removed | killed_lifetime | killed_oom
   525	    # Phase 64-A S4: handle to the lifetime-cap watchdog so stop_container
   526	    # can cancel it cleanly when the agent finishes naturally.
   527	    lifetime_task: object | None = None  # asyncio.Task
   528	    # M1: handle to the OOM watchdog (similar lifecycle to lifetime_task).
   529	    oom_task: object | None = None  # asyncio.Task
   530	    # M1: tenant_id + tokens used for this container, recorded so the
   531	    # OOM watchdog and audit trail can attribute the kill correctly.
   532	    tenant_id: str = "t-default"
   533	    tenant_budget: float = 0.0
   534	    cpus: str = ""
   535	    memory: str = ""
   536	    cpu_shares: int = 1024

   542	
   543	async def _run(cmd: str, timeout: int = DOCKER_TIMEOUT) -> tuple[int, str, str]:
   544	    """Run a shell command, return (returncode, stdout, stderr)."""
   545	    proc = await asyncio.create_subprocess_shell(
   546	        cmd,
   547	        stdout=asyncio.subprocess.PIPE,
   548	        stderr=asyncio.subprocess.PIPE,
   549	    )
   550	    stdout, stderr = await asyncio.wait_for(proc.communicate(), timeout=timeout)
   551	    return (
   552	        proc.returncode or 0,
   553	        stdout.decode(errors="replace").strip(),
   554	        stderr.decode(errors="replace").strip(),
   555	    )
   556	
   557	
   558	async def ensure_image() -> bool:
   559	    """Build the agent Docker image if it doesn't exist. Returns True if ready."""
   560	    rc, out, _ = await _run(f"docker image inspect {DOCKER_IMAGE} 2>/dev/null")
   561	    if rc == 0:
   562	        logger.info("Docker image %s already exists", DOCKER_IMAGE)
   563	        return True
   564	
   565	    emit_pipeline_phase("docker_build", f"Building agent image: {DOCKER_IMAGE}")
   566	
   567	    dockerfile = Path(__file__).parent / "docker" / "Dockerfile.agent"
   568	    if not dockerfile.exists():
   569	        logger.error("Dockerfile not found: %s", dockerfile)
   570	        return False
   571	
   572	    rc, out, err = await _run(
   573	        f"docker build -t {DOCKER_IMAGE} -f {dockerfile} {dockerfile.parent}",
   574	        timeout=BUILD_TIMEOUT,
   575	    )
   576	    if rc != 0:
   577	        logger.error("Docker build failed: %s", err or out)
   578	        emit_pipeline_phase("docker_build_error", err[:300])
   579	        return False
   580	
   581	    emit_pipeline_phase("docker_build_done", f"Image {DOCKER_IMAGE} built successfully")
   582	    return True
   583

   585	async def start_container(agent_id: str, workspace_path: Path,
   586	                          *, tier: str = "t1",
   587	                          tenant_id: str | None = None,
   588	                          tenant_budget: float | None = None) -> ContainerInfo:
   589	    """Start a Docker container for an agent with its workspace mounted.
   590	
   591	    The workspace directory is bind-mounted to /workspace inside the
   592	    container. `tier` selects the network policy:
   593	
   594	      * ``"t1"`` (default): air-gap by default, optional T1 egress
   595	        whitelist via OMNISIGHT_T1_ALLOW_EGRESS double-gate.
   596	      * ``"networked"``: place container on the omnisight-egress-t2
   597	        bridge with public-internet egress (RFC1918 still DROPped by
   598	        the host iptables script). Caller is responsible for any
   599	        Decision-Engine gate (sandbox/networked, severity=risky).
   600	
   601	    M1 — Resource hard isolation:
   602	
   603	      * ``tenant_id``: stamped as a docker label so M4 cgroup metrics
   604	        can aggregate per-tenant usage and the OOM watchdog can attach
   605	        the right tenant to ``sandbox.oom`` audit rows. Defaults to
   606	        ``current_tenant_id()`` from the request context, then
   607	        ``"t-default"``.
   608	      * ``tenant_budget``: DRF tokens (SandboxCostWeight) the I6 layer
   609	        granted this launch. Translated by ``_compute_resource_limits``
   610	        into ``--cpus`` (CFS quota cap), ``--memory`` (OOM trigger),
   611	        and ``--cpu-shares`` (cgroup v2 cpu.weight proportional share).
   612	        ``None`` = legacy settings.docker_*_limit fallback.
   613	    """
   614	    container_name = f"omnisight-agent-{agent_id}"
   615	
   616	    # Stop existing container if any
   617	    if agent_id in _containers:
   618	        await stop_container(agent_id)
   619	
   620	    # M2: hard-quota gate. If the tenant is at/over their disk quota,

     1	"""Phase 56 — workflow run inspection + resume API.
     2	
     3	GET    /workflow/runs                  list runs (filter by status)
     4	GET    /workflow/runs/{id}             replay (run + steps + in_flight)
     5	GET    /workflow/in-flight             shortcut for status=running
     6	POST   /workflow/runs/{id}/resume      mark + return; caller-side
     7	                                        re-invocation re-uses cached steps
     8	POST   /workflow/runs/{id}/finish      manually mark completed/failed
     9	                                        (admin / debug)
    10	
    11	The actual "resume" semantic lives in `backend.workflow.step` — when
    12	the caller re-runs the original code path, each `@step(run, key)`
    13	checks for a cached output before re-invoking. This router just gives
    14	operators visibility + a way to flag a run as needing attention.
    15	"""
    16	
    17	from __future__ import annotations
    18	
    19	from typing import Any

    22	from pydantic import BaseModel

    24	from backend import workflow as wf

    27	router = APIRouter(prefix="/workflow", tags=["workflow"])

    29	# Q.3-SUB-1 (#297): cross-device SSE push for workflow_run state
    30	# changes is fired inside ``backend.workflow`` itself (finish /
    31	# cancel_run / retry_run / update_run_metadata all call
    32	# ``_emit_workflow_updated_safe`` after a successful version bump).
    33	# The HTTP handlers below therefore intentionally do NOT emit a
    34	# second time — doing so would double-fire every user-driven path.

    37	def _parse_if_match(if_match: str | None) -> int:
    38	    if if_match is None:
    39	        raise HTTPException(status_code=428, detail="If-Match header required")
    40	    try:
    41	        return int(if_match.strip('" '))
    42	    except (ValueError, TypeError):
    43	        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="If-Match must be an integer version")

   107	@router.get("/runs", response_model=WorkflowRunsResponse)
   108	async def list_runs(status: str | None = None, limit: int = _pg.Limit(default=50, max_cap=200)) -> dict[str, Any]:
   109	    runs = await wf.list_runs(status=status, limit=limit)
   110	    return {
   111	        "runs": [
   112	            {
   113	                "id": r.id, "kind": r.kind, "status": r.status,
   114	                "started_at": r.started_at, "completed_at": r.completed_at,
   115	                "last_step_id": r.last_step_id, "metadata": r.metadata,
   116	                "version": r.version,
   117	            }
   118	            for r in runs
   119	        ],
   120	        "count": len(runs),
   121	    }
   122

   132	    ], "count": len(runs)}

   135	@router.get("/runs/{run_id}", response_model=WorkflowReplayResponse)
   136	async def replay_run(run_id: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
   137	    payload = await wf.replay(run_id)
   138	    if not payload:
   139	        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"workflow run {run_id} not found")
   140	    return payload
   141	
   142	
   143	@router.post("/runs/{run_id}/finish", response_model=WorkflowStatusResponse)
   144	async def finish_run(run_id: str, status: str = "completed",
   145	                     if_match: str | None = Header(None, alias="If-Match")) -> dict[str, str]:
   146	    if status not in {"completed", "failed", "halted"}:
   147	        raise HTTPException(status_code=422, detail="status must be completed|failed|halted")
   148	    run = await wf.get_run(run_id)
   149	    if not run:
   150	        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"workflow run {run_id} not found")
   151	    expected = _parse_if_match(if_match) if if_match is not None else None
   152	    try:
   153	        await wf.finish(run_id, status=status, expected_version=expected)  # type: ignore[arg-type]
   154	    except wf.VersionConflict:
   155	        raise HTTPException(status_code=409, detail="version conflict — resource modified elsewhere")
   156	    return {"id": run_id, "status": status}
   157	
   158	
   159	@router.post("/runs/{run_id}/retry", response_model=WorkflowVersionedStatusResponse)
   160	async def retry_run(run_id: str,
   161	                    if_match: str | None = Header(None, alias="If-Match")) -> dict[str, Any]:
   162	    expected = _parse_if_match(if_match)
   163	    run = await wf.get_run(run_id)
   164	    if not run:
   165	        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"workflow run {run_id} not found")
   166	    if run.status not in {"failed", "halted"}:
   167	        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="only failed/halted runs can be retried")
   168	    try:
   169	        updated = await wf.retry_run(run_id, expected)
   170	    except wf.VersionConflict:
   171	        raise HTTPException(status_code=409, detail="version conflict — resource modified elsewhere")
   172	    return {"id": updated.id, "status": updated.status, "version": updated.version}
   173	
   174	
   175	@router.post("/runs/{run_id}/cancel", response_model=WorkflowVersionedStatusResponse)
   176	async def cancel_run(run_id: str,
   177	                     if_match: str | None = Header(None, alias="If-Match")) -> dict[str, Any]:
   178	    expected = _parse_if_match(if_match)
   179	    run = await wf.get_run(run_id)
   180	    if not run:
   181	        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"workflow run {run_id} not found")
   182	    if run.status != "running":
   183	        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="only running runs can be cancelled")
   184	    try:
   185	        new_ver = await wf.cancel_run(run_id, expected)
   186	    except wf.VersionConflict:
   187	        raise HTTPException(status_code=409, detail="version conflict — resource modified elsewhere")
   188	    return {"id": run_id, "status": "halted", "version": new_ver}
   189

   193

   196	async def update_run(run_id: str, body: _MetadataUpdate,

   198	    expected = _parse_if_match(if_match)

  2577	#
  2578	# The wizard's Step 5 proves the install is coherent end-to-end by
  2579	# validating + persisting two fixed DAG payloads:
  2580	#
  2581	#   * DAG #1 ``smoke-compile-flash-host-native`` — 2 tasks
  2582	#     (``compile`` t1/cmake + ``flash`` t3/python3). On host_native the
  2583	#     t3 resolver picks LOCAL so the symbolic "flash" task is tier-
  2584	#     legal; there's no physical board to write.
  2585	#   * DAG #2 ``smoke-cross-compile-aarch64`` — validates the cross-
  2586	#     compile DAG shape + runs the t3 resolver probe for aarch64; NO
  2587	#     cross-compile actually runs.
  2588	#
  2589	# On top of the DAG results we verify the audit-log Merkle hash chain
  2590	# per tenant — if the chain is already corrupted this early, bootstrap
  2591	# is lying and the operator must know before finalize.
  2592	#
  2593	# Scope + runtime (IMPORTANT — historical comments overstated this):
  2594	#
  2595	#   What this does  : validate 2 DAGs through dag_validator (8
  2596	#                     pure-CPU rules × 2 DAGs) + workflow.start for
  2597	#                     each (INSERT workflow_run + INSERT dag_plan +
  2598	#                     UPDATE status → "executing") + walk each
  2599	#                     tenant's audit_log row-by-row to verify the
  2600	#                     hash chain.
  2601	#   What this is NOT: an execution of anything. ``set_status
  2602	#                     (plan.id, "executing")`` is a DB-metadata
  2603	#                     transition — no worker / agent picks up the
  2604	#                     plan from this call. No gcc, no cmake, no
  2605	#                     cross-toolchain, no I/O outside SQLite.
  2606	#   Expected wall   : ~1-3 seconds on healthy SQLite + reasonable
  2607	#                     audit_log size. Ballooning to minutes requires
  2608	#                     audit_log > ~10 M rows; hours is not reachable
  2609	#                     by any code path here. The "~60s" wording in
  2610	#                     earlier revisions was aspirational/stale; it
  2611	#                     does not reflect the shipped code and has been
  2612	#                     removed.
  2613	#
  2614	# Two outcomes the wizard UI needs:
  2615	#   * green — both DAGs validate + persist AND ``audit.verify_chain``
  2616	#             is intact → mark ``smoke_passed=true`` + record ``STEP_SMOKE``
  2617	#             so the fifth gate flips and finalize becomes reachable.
  2618	#   * red   — return the validator errors / audit first-bad-id so the UI
  2619	#             can render a diagnosable banner and send the operator back
  2620	#             to the appropriate earlier step.
  2621	#
  2622	# Implementation notes:
  2623	#   * The DAG payloads are byte-identical to the ones in
  2624	#     ``scripts/prod_smoke_test.py`` — single source of truth lives in
  2625	#     ``_smoke_dag_catalogue`` so prod-UI smoke and wizard smoke can't
  2626	#     drift.
  2627	#   * We do NOT shell out to the script. During bootstrap the admin has
  2628	#     not logged in yet, so the script's operator-gated ``POST /dag``
  2629	#     would 401. Instead we import the validator + workflow machinery
  2630	#     directly and let the bootstrap-gate middleware keep the route

  2637	class SmokeSubsetRequest(BaseModel):
  2638	    """Body for ``POST /bootstrap/smoke-subset``.
  2639	
  2640	    ``subset`` selects which DAG(s) to invoke. Both DAGs are
  2641	    validation + persistence only — no compile, no flash, no
  2642	    cross-compile actually executes. Wall time is dominated by
  2643	    the per-tenant audit-chain walk and clocks in at ~1-3s on a
  2644	    healthy stack; see the section comment above for the full
  2645	    scope breakdown.
  2646	
  2647	      * ``dag1`` (default) — the compile-flash host_native DAG.
  2648	      * ``dag2`` — the aarch64 cross-compile DAG.
  2649	      * ``both`` — run ``dag1`` + ``dag2`` sequentially so the Step-5
  2650	        UI can render a run summary for each DAG shipped in
  2651	        ``scripts/prod_smoke_test.py``.
  2652	
  2653	    The wizard drives ``subset="both"`` so the operator sees a run
  2654	    summary per DAG; external callers / CLI users can still pin to
  2655	    ``dag1`` for a slightly quicker single-DAG signal.

  2659	        default="dag1",
  2660	        description=(

  2662	            "host_native), ``dag2`` (aarch64 cross-compile), or "

  2699	def _dag1_payload() -> dict:
  2700	    """Return the compile-flash host_native DAG payload verbatim.
  2701	
  2702	    Mirrors ``DAG_1_COMPILE_FLASH_HOST_NATIVE`` in ``scripts/prod_smoke_test.py``
  2703	    so the two smoke invocations test the same artefact. Kept as a
  2704	    function (not a module-level dict) so a test can swap in a smaller
  2705	    stub without mutating global state.
  2706	    """
  2707	    return {
  2708	        "dag": {
  2709	            "schema_version": 1,
  2710	            "dag_id": _SMOKE_DAG_ID,
  2711	            "tasks": [
  2712	                {
  2713	                    "task_id": "compile",
  2714	                    "description": (
  2715	                        "Build firmware image (host-native, no cross-compile)"
  2716	                    ),
  2717	                    "required_tier": "t1",
  2718	                    "toolchain": "cmake",
  2719	                    "inputs": [],
  2720	                    "expected_output": "build/firmware.bin",
  2721	                    "depends_on": [],
  2722	                },
  2723	                {
  2724	                    # On host_native the T3 resolver picks LOCAL and the
  2725	                    # validator swaps the effective tier to t1. python3 is
  2726	                    # a t1-legal toolchain so the symbolic "flash" step
  2727	                    # passes validation — there's no physical board to
  2728	                    # flash when target arch == host arch.
  2729	                    "task_id": "flash",
  2730	                    "description": (
  2731	                        "Flash built image (T3 resolves to LOCAL on host_native)"
  2732	                    ),
  2733	                    "required_tier": "t3",
  2734	                    "toolchain": "python3",
  2735	                    "inputs": ["build/firmware.bin"],
  2736	                    "expected_output": "logs/flash.log",
  2737	                    "depends_on": ["compile"],
  2738	                },
  2739	            ],
  2740	        },
  2741	        "target_platform": "host_native",
  2742	        "metadata": {"source": "bootstrap:smoke-subset", "test_run": True},
  2743	    }
  2744	
  2745	
  2746	def _dag2_payload() -> dict:
  2747	    """Return the cross-compile aarch64 DAG payload verbatim.
  2748	
  2749	    Mirrors ``DAG_2_CROSS_COMPILE_AARCH64`` in ``scripts/prod_smoke_test.py``.
  2750	    During bootstrap we validate + persist the plan only — the actual
  2751	    aarch64 cross-compile toolchain is not invoked in-process, so the
  2752	    DAG completes the ``workflow.start`` handshake almost instantly on
  2753	    any host regardless of whether ``aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc`` is present.
  2754	    """
  2755	    return {
  2756	        "dag": {
  2757	            "schema_version": 1,
  2758	            "dag_id": _SMOKE_DAG_ID_AARCH64,
  2759	            "tasks": [
  2760	                {
  2761	                    "task_id": "cross-compile",
  2762	                    "description": (
  2763	                        "Cross-compile firmware for AArch64 target"
  2764	                    ),
  2765	                    "required_tier": "t1",
  2766	                    "toolchain": "cmake",
  2767	                    "inputs": [],
  2768	                    "expected_output": "build/firmware-aarch64.bin",
  2769	                    "depends_on": [],
  2770	                },
  2771	                {
  2772	                    "task_id": "package",
  2773	                    "description": (
  2774	                        "Package cross-compiled artifact for deployment"
  2775	                    ),
  2776	                    "required_tier": "t1",
  2777	                    "toolchain": "make",
  2778	                    "inputs": ["build/firmware-aarch64.bin"],
  2779	                    "expected_output": "dist/firmware-aarch64.tar.gz",
  2780	                    "depends_on": ["cross-compile"],
  2781	                },
  2782	            ],
  2783	        },
  2784	        "target_platform": "aarch64",
  2785	        "metadata": {"source": "bootstrap:smoke-subset", "test_run": True},
  2786	    }
  2787	
  2788	
  2789	# Single source of truth for the bootstrap smoke DAG catalogue. Matches
  2790	# the (key, label, payload) tuple shape used by
  2791	# ``scripts/prod_smoke_test.py`` so the two surfaces cannot drift.
  2792	def _smoke_dag_catalogue() -> list[tuple[str, str, str, dict]]:
  2793	    return [
  2794	        (
  2795	            "dag1",
  2796	            "DAG #1: compile-flash (host_native)",
  2797	            _SMOKE_DAG_ID,
  2798	            _dag1_payload(),
  2799	        ),
  2800	        (
  2801	            "dag2",
  2802	            "DAG #2: cross-compile (aarch64)",
  2803	            _SMOKE_DAG_ID_AARCH64,
  2804	            _dag2_payload(),
  2805	        ),
  2806	    ]
  2807	
  2808	
  2809	def _select_smoke_dags(subset: str) -> list[tuple[str, str, str, dict]]:
  2810	    """Filter the catalogue by subset keyword (mirrors the CLI helper)."""
  2811	    catalogue = _smoke_dag_catalogue()
  2812	    if subset == "both":
  2813	        return catalogue
  2814	    return [entry for entry in catalogue if entry[0] == subset]
  2815	
  2816	
  2817	async def _run_smoke_dag(
  2818	    key: str, label: str, dag_id: str, payload: dict,
  2819	) -> SmokeRunSummary:
  2820	    """Validate + open a workflow run for one smoke DAG payload.
  2821	
  2822	    Generic replacement for the old ``_run_dag1_smoke`` helper — the
  2823	    shape is identical so the wizard's Step-5 pane can iterate over
  2824	    ``runs[]`` without special-casing any individual DAG.
  2825	    """
  2826	    from backend.dag_schema import DAG
  2827	    from backend import dag_storage as _ds
  2828	    from backend import dag_validator as _dv
  2829	    from backend import workflow as wf
  2830	    from backend.routers import dag as _dag_router
  2831	
  2832	    target_name = payload.get("target_platform") or "host_native"
  2833	
  2834	    try:
  2835	        dag = DAG.model_validate(payload["dag"])
  2836	    except Exception as exc:
  2837	        return SmokeRunSummary(
  2838	            key=key,
  2839	            label=label,
  2840	            dag_id=dag_id,
  2841	            ok=False,
  2842	            validation_errors=[{
  2843	                "rule": "schema",
  2844	                "task_id": None,
  2845	                "message": str(exc),
  2846	            }],
  2847	            target_platform=target_name,
  2848	        )
  2849	
  2850	    target_profile = _dag_router._resolve_target_profile(target_name)
  2851	    result = _dv.validate(dag, target_profile=target_profile)
  2852	
  2853	    try:
  2854	        from backend.t3_resolver import resolve_from_profile
  2855	
  2856	        t3_resolution = resolve_from_profile(target_profile).kind.value
  2857	    except Exception as exc:
  2858	        logger.debug("bootstrap: smoke t3 resolver probe failed: %s", exc)
  2859	        t3_resolution = None
  2860	
  2861	    if not result.ok:
  2862	        return SmokeRunSummary(
  2863	            key=key,
  2864	            label=label,
  2865	            dag_id=dag_id,
  2866	            ok=False,
  2867	            validation_errors=[e.to_dict() for e in result.errors],
  2868	            plan_status="failed",
  2869	            task_count=len(dag.tasks),
  2870	            t3_runner=t3_resolution,
  2871	            target_platform=target_name,
  2872	        )
  2873	
  2874	    metadata = dict(payload.get("metadata") or {})
  2875	    metadata.setdefault("source", "bootstrap:smoke-subset")
  2876	    metadata.setdefault("target_platform", target_name)
  2877	
  2878	    try:
  2879	        run = await wf.start(
  2880	            "invoke",
  2881	            dag=dag,
  2882	            metadata=metadata,
  2883	            target_profile=target_profile,
  2884	        )
  2885	    except Exception as exc:
  2886	        logger.exception("bootstrap: smoke workflow.start failed")
  2887	        return SmokeRunSummary(
  2888	            key=key,
  2889	            label=label,
  2890	            dag_id=dag_id,
  2891	            ok=False,
  2892	            validation_errors=[{
  2893	                "rule": "workflow_start",
  2894	                "task_id": None,
  2895	                "message": f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"[:400],
  2896	            }],
  2897	            task_count=len(dag.tasks),
  2898	            t3_runner=t3_resolution,
  2899	            target_platform=target_name,
  2900	        )
  2901	
  2902	    plan = await _ds.get_plan_by_run(run.id)
  2903	    plan_status = plan.status if plan else None
  2904	    plan_id = plan.id if plan else None
  2905	    smoke_ok = bool(plan and plan_status in ("validated", "executing"))
  2906	
  2907	    return SmokeRunSummary(
  2908	        key=key,
  2909	        label=label,
  2910	        dag_id=dag_id,
  2911	        ok=smoke_ok,
  2912	        validation_errors=(plan.errors() if plan and not smoke_ok else []),
  2913	        run_id=run.id,
  2914	        plan_id=plan_id,
  2915	        plan_status=plan_status,
  2916	        task_count=len(dag.tasks),
  2917	        t3_runner=t3_resolution,
  2918	        target_platform=target_name,
  2919	    )
  2920	
  2921	
  2922	async def _verify_audit_chain() -> AuditChainSummary:
  2923	    """Verify every tenant's audit hash chain and aggregate the result.
  2924	
  2925	    Matches the ``/audit/verify-all`` semantics the prod smoke script
  2926	    reaches for, but called in-process so it works during bootstrap
  2927	    before any admin session exists.
  2928	    """
  2929	    try:
  2930	        from backend import audit as _audit
  2931	
  2932	        results = await _audit.verify_all_chains()
  2933	    except Exception as exc:
  2934	        logger.exception("bootstrap: audit verify_all_chains failed")
  2935	        return AuditChainSummary(
  2936	            ok=False,
  2937	            first_bad_id=None,
  2938	            detail=f"verify_all_chains raised: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"[:300],
  2939	            tenant_count=0,
  2940	            bad_tenants=[],
  2941	        )
  2942	
  2943	    first_bad: int | None = None
  2944	    bad_tenants: list[str] = []
  2945	    for tid, (ok, bad) in sorted(results.items()):
  2946	        if not ok:
  2947	            bad_tenants.append(tid)
  2948	            if first_bad is None:
  2949	                first_bad = bad
  2950	    all_ok = not bad_tenants
  2951	    detail = (
  2952	        f"{len(results)} tenant(s) verified"
  2953	        if all_ok
  2954	        else f"broken chain in tenant(s): {', '.join(bad_tenants)}"
  2955	    )
  2956	    return AuditChainSummary(
  2957	        ok=all_ok,
  2958	        first_bad_id=first_bad,
  2959	        detail=detail,
  2960	        tenant_count=len(results),
  2961	        bad_tenants=bad_tenants,
  2962	    )
  2963	
  2964	
  2965	@router.post("/smoke-subset", response_model=SmokeSubsetResponse)
  2966	async def bootstrap_smoke_subset(
  2967	    req: SmokeSubsetRequest | None = None,
  2968	) -> SmokeSubsetResponse:
  2969	    """Run the wizard's Step-5 smoke subset (compile-flash host_native and/or
  2970	    cross-compile aarch64).
  2971	
  2972	    Sequence:
  2973	      1. Validate + submit each selected DAG via ``workflow.start`` —
  2974	         same artefact shape the prod-UI smoke uses. ``subset=both``
  2975	         yields one run summary per DAG so Step 5 can render both.
  2976	      2. Verify the audit hash chain for every tenant (catches
  2977	         pre-finalize tampering).
  2978	      3. On every selected DAG green AND audit chain intact, flip the
  2979	         ``smoke_passed`` bootstrap marker + record ``STEP_SMOKE`` so
  2980	         the finalize gate can pass.
  2981	
  2982	    Unauthenticated like every other wizard endpoint — the bootstrap-
  2983	    gate middleware keeps ``/bootstrap/*`` wizard-scoped until finalize.
  2984	    Response is always HTTP 200 — ``smoke_passed`` in the body carries
  2985	    the outcome so the UI can render the result pane without parsing
  2986	    error bodies.
  2987	    """
  2988	    body = req or SmokeSubsetRequest()
  2989	    started = time.monotonic()
  2990	
  2991	    selected = _select_smoke_dags(body.subset)
  2992	    run_summaries: list[SmokeRunSummary] = []
  2993	    for key, label, dag_id, payload in selected:
  2994	        run_summaries.append(await _run_smoke_dag(key, label, dag_id, payload))
  2995

   890	async def start_networked_container(agent_id: str, workspace_path: Path,
   891	                                    *, tenant_id: str | None = None,
   892	                                    tenant_budget: float | None = None) -> ContainerInfo:
   893	    """Phase 64-B convenience wrapper. Equivalent to
   894	    ``start_container(..., tier="networked")``. Use this from MLOps /
   895	    third-party-API agent paths *after* the caller has cleared the
   896	    Decision Engine ``sandbox/networked`` gate (severity=risky)."""
   897	    return await start_container(

   903	async def start_t3_local_container(agent_id: str, workspace_path: Path,
   904	                                   *, tenant_id: str | None = None,
   905	                                   tenant_budget: float | None = None) -> ContainerInfo:
   906	    """Phase 64-C-LOCAL S2 — T3 executor for the host==target path.
   907	
   908	    Used by the T3 runner when `t3_resolver.resolve_t3_runner()` has
   909	    picked the LOCAL kind: same arch, same OS, so a binary built in
   910	    this sandbox can run on the host. Concretely, `--network host`
   911	    so smoke-tests against localhost / systemd-managed services work,
   912	    plus the same runsc/workspace envelope T1 ships with.
   913	
   914	    Callers should go through `t3_resolver.resolve_t3_runner()` rather
   915	    than invoking this directly — it records the metric and delegates.
   916	
   917	    Deploy actions that need to mutate host state (systemctl enable,
   918	    write to /etc/nginx/, etc.) should still NOT happen inside this
   919	    container. The pattern is: agent produces artefact + install.sh
   920	    in /workspace, orchestrator runs install.sh on the host (outside
   921	    the sandbox) as a separate pipeline step. That keeps the sandbox
   922	    a containment boundary even on the happy-path.
   923	    """
   924	    return await start_container(
   925	        agent_id, workspace_path, tier="t3-local",
   926	        tenant_id=tenant_id, tenant_budget=tenant_budget,
   927	    )
   928	
   929	
   930	async def dispatch_t3(
   931	    agent_id: str,
   932	    workspace_path: Path,
   933	    target_arch: str = "",
   934	    target_os: str = "linux",
   935	    *,
   936	    tenant_id: str | None = None,
   937	    tenant_budget: float | None = None,
   938	) -> "tuple[ContainerInfo | None, T3RunnerKind]":  # noqa: F821
   939	    """Phase 64-C-LOCAL S2 — single entry point for the T3 dispatcher.
   940	
   941	    Consults the resolver, bumps the prometheus counter so the Ops
   942	    Summary panel (S4) can show a live runner distribution, and
   943	    returns (ContainerInfo, RunnerKind). Returns (None, BUNDLE) when
   944	    no live runner is available yet — caller is expected to fall
   945	    back to the artefact-bundle path.
   946	
   947	    Extending to SSH/QEMU is additive in this function: as each kind
   948	    gains a backing runner, add a branch here; call sites don't change.
   949	    """
   950	    from backend.t3_resolver import (
   951	        T3RunnerKind, record_dispatch, resolve_t3_runner,
   952	    )
   953	    res = resolve_t3_runner(target_arch, target_os)
   954	    record_dispatch(res.kind)
   955	    logger.info(
   956	        "dispatch_t3: agent=%s target=%s/%s → %s (%s)",
   957	        agent_id, res.target_arch or "?", res.target_os,
   958	        res.kind.value, res.reason,
   959	    )
   960	    if res.kind == T3RunnerKind.LOCAL:
   961	        info = await start_t3_local_container(
   962	            agent_id, workspace_path,
   963	            tenant_id=tenant_id, tenant_budget=tenant_budget,
   964	        )
   965	        return info, res.kind
   966	    if res.kind == T3RunnerKind.SSH:
   967	        from backend.ssh_runner import find_target_for_arch, SSHRunnerInfo
   968	        ssh_target = find_target_for_arch(res.target_arch, res.target_os)
   969	        if ssh_target is not None:
   970	            ssh_info = SSHRunnerInfo(
   971	                agent_id=agent_id,
   972	                target=ssh_target,
   973	                status="ready",
   974	            )
   975	            return ssh_info, res.kind
   976	        logger.warning(
   977	            "dispatch_t3: resolver said SSH but no target found for %s/%s",
   978	            res.target_arch, res.target_os,
   979	        )
   980	        return None, T3RunnerKind.BUNDLE
   981	    # BUNDLE / QEMU (64-C-QEMU): no live runner yet; caller handles
   982	    # the bundling / remote dispatch at the orchestrator layer.
   983	    return None, res.kind
   984

   990	    *,

     9	
    10	Two suites, each run by its own systemd timer against the develop-tip

    12	
    13	  ``canary``  exercises the staging HTTP surface (liveness + readiness
    14	              probes) and appends a ``{"suite": "canary", ...}`` line to
    15	              ``canary-status.jsonl``.
    16	  ``smoke``   runs ``scripts/prod_smoke_test.py`` against the staging base
    17	              URL and appends a ``{"suite": "smoke", ...}`` line to
    18	              ``smoke-status.jsonl``. ``release_milestone_checker`` requires
    19	              the smoke status be < 4h old, so the timer cadence stays well
    20	              under that (see ``deploy/systemd/staging-gate-smoke.timer``).
    21	
    22	Record shape — must satisfy ``JsonlStatusReader.latest``
    23	(``release_milestone_checker.py:296-334``)::
    24	
    25	  {"suite": "canary"|"smoke", "status": "green"|"red", "branch": "develop",
    26	   "revision": "<develop tip sha>", "run_id": "...", "timestamp": "...Z",
    27	   "detail": "...", "bundle_id": "...",

    29	   "observed_api_version": {...}}
    30	
    31	``status`` is ``green`` only when the suite fully passed. ``release_milestone
    32	_checker`` treats anything outside {green, ok, pass, passed, success} as a
    33	blocked gate, which is the conservative behaviour we want on failure.
    34

    39	The audit write never blocks or fails the gate run.
    40	
    41	§5 operator override — when a producer goes red the operator can still ship
    42	via the ``release:force-promote`` mechanism (ADR-0019 / AUDIT-18, OP-966).
    43	This module only *reports* status — it never decides promotion — so it never
    44	interferes with the override path.
    45	
    46	Exit codes (``main``)
    47	---------------------
    48	``0``  suite green — JSONL line + audit row written.
    49	``2``  suite red — JSONL line still written (so the gate sees the red), then
    50	       exit non-zero so the systemd ``OnFailure=`` alert hook fires.
    51	``3``  configuration / environment error (e.g. cannot resolve develop tip) —
